The Synthetic Hilbert Additive Group Scheme
Alice Hedenlund, Tasos Moulinos

Abstract
. We construct a lift of the degree filtration on the integer-valued polynomials to (even MU \MU -based) synthetic spectra. Namely, we construct a bialgebra in modules over the evenly filtered sphere spectrum which base-changes to the degree filtration on the integer-valued polynomials. As a consequence, we may lift the Hilbert additive group scheme to a spectral group scheme over A 1 / G m \mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m} . We study the cohomology of its deloopings, and show that one obtains a lift of the filtered circle, studied in [ 22 ] . At the level of quasi-coherent sheaves, one obtains synthetic lifts of the Z \mathbb{Z} -linear ∞ \infty -categories of S fil 1 S^{1}_{\fil} -representations. Our constructions crucially rely on the use of the even filtration of Hahn–Raksit–Wilson; it is linearity with respect to the even filtered sphere that powers the results of this work.



Introduction

Background and Aim

The ring of integer–valued polynomials
\Int(\mathbb{Z})=\{f\in\mathbb{Q}[x]|f(\mathbb{Z})\subset\mathbb{Z}\}
is the free binomial ring on one generator.
This carries a natural bicommutative bialgebra structure, and so we obtain a group scheme

\mathbb{H}=\mathrm{Spec}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))

over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
which we will refer to as the
Hilbert additive group scheme
.
This object was studied by Toën in
[
30
]
in the context of the schematization program of Grothendieck, and more recently in the works of Antieau, Horel, and Kubrak–Shuklin–Zakharov in the context of integral models for spaces (cf.
[
2
,
14
,
15
]
).
It has been suggested that
\mathbb{H}
along with its additional structure as a sheaf of rings could play the role of a structure sheaf for a deeper geometric base than
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.
This is partially substantiated by the existence of a canonical ring map

\mathbb{H}\to\mathcal{O}

of fpqc sheaves over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
, where
\mathcal{O}
denotes the standard structure sheaf representing global sections.

The ring of integer–valued polynomials admits a natural filtration; as a subring of the polynomial ring in one variable over
\mathbb{Q}
, it inherits the filtration by degree.
We will denote this filtration by
\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))
.
We remark that this filtration has already played a rather significant role in the literature.
Indeed, via the dictionary between filtrations and quasi-coherent sheaves over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, a paradigm proposed by Simpson
[
28
]
and later set up in the modern
\infty
-categorical setting by the second author
[
23
]
, one obtains a stack

\mathbb{H}_{\fil}=\mathrm{fSpec}(\fil_{\deg}^{*}\Int(\mathbb{Z}))

over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We refer to this as the
filtered Hilbert additive group scheme
.
The classifying stack

S^{1}_{\fil}=B_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\mathbb{H}_{\fil}

was considered in
[
22
]
under the moniker the
filtered circle
1
1

1

This was considered in the
p
-local setting in loc. cit.
.
The formalism of derived algebraic geometry provides a geometrization of the Hochschild–Konsant–Rosenberg (HKR) filtration, together with its compatible
S^{1}
-equivariant structure. Moreover, for an arbitrary derived scheme, its HKR-filtered Hochschild homology acquires a universal property as a filtered
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra with an
S^{1}_{\fil}
-action.

Suppose now that we want to endow the motivic filtration on
topological Hochschild homology
with a similar universal property.
A key step in doing so entails lifting the filtered circle, or equivalently the filtered Hilbert additive group, to some setting of algebraic geometry over the sphere spectrum.
This is exactly what we do in this paper.
Namely, we lift the filtered Hilbert additive group to an abelian group object in spectral algebraic geometry; we call this the
synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme
.

Forgetting filtrations for a moment, the ring of integer–valued polynomials certainly has a lift to the spectral setting.
Indeed, such a lift is given by using the spherical Witt vectors
[
18
,
5
,
2
]
.
Recall that the spherical Witt vectors functor

\mathbb{SW}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\longrightarrow\CAlg_{\mathbb{S}_{p}}^{\mathrm{cn},\wedge}

assigns to a perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra a connective
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra.
At this point, we note that the ring of integer–valued polynomials is a perfect commutative ring in the sense that
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
is perfect over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
for all primes
p
.
Hence, we can apply the spherical Witt vector construction and glue this over all primes via the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p}\mathbb{SW}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p}\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{p}\,.}

We refer to the pullback
\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
as the
spherical integer–valued polynomials
.
The construction
\mathbb{S}_{(-)}:\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{perf}}\to\CAlg(\Sp)
which assigns an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring to a perfect commutative ring was already mentioned and discussed in in
[
2
]
*Section 7.
The spherical integer–valued polynomials is a flat lift of the integer–valued polynomials which also admits a bicommutative bialgebra structure in
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings.
We can hence make the definition

\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})})

which determines an affine group scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
that we will refer to as the
spherical Hilbert additive group scheme
.

Let us now return to the question of lifting the degree filtration
\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))
.
By the works of
[
8
,
25
]
, the degree filtration is intimately connected, by way of Cartier duality
2
2

2

Roughly speaking, Cartier duality is an equivalence of formal groups with affine group schemes; this can be lifted to the spectral setting.
, with the degeneration of the formal multiplicative group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
to the formal additive group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
.
Thus, a necessary condition for lifting the degree filtration, equivalently the degeneration
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\leadsto\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
, would be the existence of lifts of the two formal groups over the sphere spectrum.
While the multiplicative group lifts, the additive formal group manifestly does not lift to a formal group over the sphere, as is shown in
[
18
, Proposition 1.6.20]
.
Thus, it seems futile to try to find a lift of this degeneration, as the second author muses in
[
25
]
*Section 10.

We now describe the core insight that allows us to bypass the issue outlined above.
By, for example
[
23
]
, we can think of the stable
\infty
-category of filtered spectra as a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant family of stable
\infty
-categories, varying along a parameter
t
. When
t=0
, one recovers graded spectra. We instead replace this with another family, where the generic fiber is still spectra, but where, at
t=0
, the fiber
\infty
-category is
\mathbb{Z}
-linear and where consequently, the additive formal group is still well–defined.
This perspective is naturally implemented by the
\infty
-category of
synthetic spectra
.

Without going too much into specifics, (even) synthetic spectra is a deformation of the algebraic category of quasi-coherent sheaves on the moduli stack of formal groups to the
\infty
-category of spectra.
We refer the reader to
[
26
,
9
]
for more on synthetic spectra and their various incarnations.
In this paper, we will specifically work with “even
\MU
-based synthetic spectra”, and we will not think of them in a more complicated manner than as modules over the even filtration on the sphere spectrum

\mathrm{SynSp}=\Mod_{\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}(\Filt(\Sp))\,.

Recall from
[
10
]
that the even-filtration of an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
is defined as the right Kan extension of the double-speed Postnikov filtration along the inclusion of even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings into all
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings:

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(A)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\to B\\
B\text{ even}\end{subarray}}\tau_{\geq 2\ast}(B)\,.

As usual, a spectrum is called even if its homotopy groups are concentrated in even degrees.
The even filtration of the sphere spectrum is sometimes called the synthetic sphere spectrum and we will denote it by
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
.
We will refer to the natural map

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}=\fil_{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}

as the
synthetic Hurewicz map
, where we have noted that the even filtration on
\mathbb{Z}
can be identified with the trivial complete filtration on
\mathbb{Z}
.
One of the first hints that the additive Hilbert group scheme might be possible to lift over the synthetic sphere spectrum is that fact that the associated graded of the latter is
\mathbb{Z}
-linear.
This is the conceptual starting point for this project.

Main Results

Without further ado, we describe the main results of this work.

Lifting the degree filtration

The first goal of this paper is to lift the degree filtration on the integer-valued polynomials to the spectral setting, and hence give a spectral lift of the filtered Hilbert additive group scheme.
The inspiration comes in two steps.
Let us use the notation
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
and
\mathbb{T}_{\fil}=\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{T}
.
Firstly, we observe the equivalences

\Int(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}}\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad\fil^{*}_{\deg}\Int(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{(\mathbb{T}_{\fil})^{\vee}}\mathbb{Z}\,,

the first one exhibited in
[
30
]
and the second one is proven
p
-locally in
[
22
]
; we refine the latter to an integral equivalence in Proposition
5.17
.
Secondly, we observe that the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
coincides with its even filtration
3
3

3

We issue a warning for a potential pitfall here.
While it is true that

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}\quad\text{and}\quad(\mathbb{T}_{\fil})^{\vee}\simeq\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}\,,

the even filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
does not coincide with its Postnikov filtration.
Instead, the even filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
coincides with its trivial filtration.
.
Inspired by these two facts, we replace all instances
\mathbb{Z}
with
\mathbb{S}
and switch from Postnikov filtrations to the even filtration, and make the definitions

\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}=\fil_{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}=\hom_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}},\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\,,

in the category of synthetic spectra.
Both of these objects are bicommutative bialgebras in synthetic spectra, as we show in Proposition
2.11
.
The bar construction on the latter provides us with what we refer to as the synthetic lift of the integer-valued polynomials

\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}^{\mathrm{syn}}=\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\,.

This is a non-trivial filtration on
\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{S})}
which we show base-changes to the degree filtration on the integer–valued polynomials in the following sense.

Theorem 0.1
.

There is an equivalence

\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq\fil^{*}_{\deg}\Int(\mathbb{Z})

of bicommutative bialgebras in
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}}(\Filt(\Sp))
.

Having found the proper spectral lift of the degree filtration, the following definition gives us the correct spectral lift of the filtered Hilbert additive group scheme.

Notation 0.2
.

Let us write

\mathrm{fSpec}:\CAlg(\Fil(\Sp))\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\CAlg(\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}))\overset{\mathrm{Spec}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{sStk}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}

and

\mathrm{grSpec}:\CAlg(\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\CAlg(\QCoh(B\mathbb{G}_{m}))\overset{\mathrm{Spec}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{sStk}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}

where we have used the t-exact symmetric monoidal equivalences set up in
[
23
]
.

Definition 0.3
.

The
synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme
is

\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}=\mathrm{fSpec}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\,.

This is a relatively affine spectral scheme over
\mathrm{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
.
Similiarly, we define the
graded spherical Hilbert additive group scheme
to be

\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}=\mathrm{grSpec}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\,.

Because of the above theorem, this object base-changes to the filtered Hilbert additive groups scheme.

Remark 0.4
.

To give the reader a sense of how the above objects fit together, we note, following Remark
7.6
, that the synthetic Hilbert additive group fits into the following diagram of pullback squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{grSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}\,.}

In particular, we recover the spherical Hilbert additive group as the fiber of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
.

Deloopings of the synthetic Hilbert additive group

Our next goal is to give a version of the filtered circle, and its further deloopings, in the spectral setting.
Let
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
denote the classifying stack over
\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
. As taking classifying stacks is compatible with base-change, this gives a lift of the filtered circle to the setting of spectral algebraic geometry.
The following result concerns the cohomology of the stack
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.

Theorem 0.5
.

(1)

(
Cohomology of the generic fiber
) Let
\underline{S^{1}}
denote the constant spectral stack associated to the circle
S^{1}
.
Then there is a map
\underline{S^{1}}\to B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
which induces an equivalence

R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}

of bicommutative bialgebras between the cohomology of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
and the spherical cochain algebra
\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}
.

(2)

(
Cohomology of the special fiber
) There is an equivalence

R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})\simeq\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)

of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras in
\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}(\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr})
.
Here,
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
denotes the associated graded of the even filtration on the sphere and
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)
the graded spectrum obtained by desuspending and shifting this.

In addition, we show that global sections of the
n
th delooping of
\mathbb{H}^{\mathrm{syn}}
recovers
\mathbb{S}^{K(\mathbb{Z},n)}
, the spherical cochains on the Eilenberg–MacLane space
K(\mathbb{Z},n)
.
In particular, we obtain
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
-linear filtrations of
\mathbb{S}^{K(\mathbb{Z},n)}
, for all positive integers
n
.
We remark that these filtrations are not the same as the even filtration applied to these objects.

Representations of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}

We then embark on a study of the
\infty
-category of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
-representations.
Our main results in this direction can be summarized by the following statement:

Theorem 0.6
.

The map
B\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\to B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})\simeq\Fun(BS^{1},\Sp)\,.

On the special fiberwe obtain a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})\simeq\operatorname{coMod}_{\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})}(\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})})\,,

with graded comodules over
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})
.

Note that by construction,
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})
base-changes to
H^{*}(S^{1};\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)
. On the special fiber, we see therefore that we obtain a lift of the graded mixed complexes of
[
22
]
.

Remark 0.7
.

We wonder if there is a way, given these equivalences, to geometrize the theory of cyclotomic spectra and synthetic cyclotomic spectra as studied in
[
1
]
.

Some questions and further directions

We now list some questions and ideas that arose during the writing of this paper.

Lifting
\delta
- and binomial rings

In
[
30
]
, the Hilbert additive group scheme was given a Witt vector model as the fixed points of the Frobenii on the Witt vector ring scheme.
By amalgamating constructions from
[
30
]
and
[
22
]
, we extend this to a Witt vector model of the filtered Hilbert additive group in Section
5.3
.
One might wonder if Witt vector models exist over the sphere spectrum as well.
Even in the
p
-local setting, this is quite subtle; since
\mathbb{W}_{p}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\{x\})
for the free
\delta
-ring
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\{x\}
, we remark that this is equivalent to asking if the free
\delta
-ring admits a lift to the sphere spectrum.
This seems to be an open question, cf.
[
6
]
.
This question is interesting as it seems to be an instance of a recognizable pattern: when the Frobenii are trivialized, it is possible to lift.
In a related direction, the study of binomial rings, such as the integer-valued polynomials, has been extended to the derived setting in
[
15
]
.
Moreover, the degree filtration on
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
is mentioned in loc. cit. in the context of degenerations of free binomial algebras to divided power algebras.
We are lifting this degeneration to synthetic spectra for the free binomial ring on one generator.
We wonder therefore if the binomial ring monad lifts to a monad on synthetic spectra.

Affine stacks in spectral algebraic geometry

We remark that the above definition of the synthetic integer-valued polynomials as a relative tensor product is directly inspired by
[
30
, Corollaire 3.5]
.
The equivalence of loc. cit. is a direct consequence of the theory of affine stacks, c.f.
[
29
]
.
Moreover, the cohomological computations for
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
can be viewed as an instance of a bar–cobar equivalence.
Both of these phenomena underlie another key motivation of this paper: understanding what affine stacks over the sphere spectrum should be.
While there is currently no theory of affine stacks in spectral algebraic geometry, we will see that the spectral stacks we construct do exhibit some of the behavior of affine stacks, and this is what powers many of the results here.
Hence, this paper represents some steps in the direction of honing in on the right notion of affine stacks in spectral algebraic geometry.
For more on this, see the discussion in Section
8.4
.

Motivic filtrations and prismatic homotopy theory

Let us remark that we stop short of providing the motivic filtration on topological Hochschild homology with an analogous universal property to that of HKR-filtered Hochschild homology.
While the constructions of this paper do give rise to a filtration on topological Hochschild homology of any connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra, this filtration is not expected to coincide with the motivic filtration.
Indeed, we expect that spectral algebraic geometry, over connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, to be the wrong setting to formulate this.
See Section
9.2
for a description of what goes wrong.
Nevertheless, we expect that there will exist an avatar of our synthetic Hilbert additive group with its group structure in the correct setting and that it would play an essential role in the formulation of this universal property.

Terminology and Notation

•

We use the notation
\mathcal{S}
for the
\infty
-category of spaces and
\Sp
for the stable
\infty
-category of spectra.

•

We use the notation
\Map
for mapping spaces,
\map
for mapping spectra, and
\hom
for internal function objects. In particular, if
R
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring and if
M
and
N
are
R
-modules, then
\hom_{R}(M,N)
denoted the
R
-module given by the universal property

\Map(L\otimes_{R}M,N)\simeq\Map(L,\hom_{R}(M,N))

in the category
\Mod_{R}
.

1.
The Even Filtration and Even
\MU
-based Synthetic Spectra

In this section, we introduce one of the key tools and perspectives used in this paper, namely that of the functorial even filtration on
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings and synthetic spectra.
We take the point of view of even
\MU
-based synthetic spectra as modules over the even filtration on the sphere spectrum.
We begin in §1.1 with a discussion of terminology and conventions for filtrations and graded objects.
In §1.2, we introduce the even filtration following
[
10
]
, move on to the definition of the even filtration, and then in §1.3 we discuss even
\MU
-based synthetic spectra from this perspective.

1.1.
Filtered Spectra

Consider the poset
\mathbb{Z}
with its standard ordering.
We consider this as a category: its objects are integers, and the mapping set
\mathbb{Z}(m,n)
has a single element if
m\leq n
and is empty otherwise.
The
\infty
-category of filtered spectra is simply the functor category

\Filt(\Sp)=\Fun(\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{op}},\Sp)\,.

Some notations and constructions related to this category:

•

If
X
is a filtration, then the object in filtration degree
p
is denoted
X^{p}
.
Consider the functor
\Filt(\Sp)\to\Sp
which just sends a filtration to the spectrum in filtration degree
p
.
This admits a fully faithful left adjoint which we denote by
L_{p}:\Sp\to\Filt(\Sp)
.
On objects this functor is given by

(L_{p}A)^{n}=\begin{cases}A&\text{for $n\leq p$}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We will sometimes refer to filtrations on this form as
trivial
.
In particular,
L_{0}A
will sometimes be referred to as the
trivial filtration
on
A
.

•

The
\infty
-category
\Filt(\Sp)
is symmetric monoidal when endowed with the Day convolution tensor product.
This is given by the formula

(X\otimes Y)^{n}\simeq\colim_{i+j\leq n}X^{i}\otimes Y^{j}\,.

The unit of the symmetric monoidal structure is the trivial filtration
L_{0}\mathbb{S}
.
A
filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
is a commutative algebra object in
\Filt(\Sp)
with respect to this symmetric monoidal structure.
We denote the
\infty
-category of filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings by
\CAlg(\Filt)
.

•

We will write
(k):\Filt(\Sp)\to\Filt(\Sp)
to denote the functor that shifts a filtered object
k
times:

(X(k))^{n}=X^{n-k}\,.

•

The
\infty
-category
\Filt(\Sp)
can be endowed with a number of different t-structures.
In this paper, we will often mention the so-called
neutral
t-structure. The connective objects in this t-structure are filtrations
X
such that
X^{n}\in\Sp_{\geq 0}
for all
n
.

•

A filtration
X
is called
complete
if
\lim_{n}X^{n}\simeq 0
.

Example 1.1

(The double-speed Postnikov filtration)
.

A common example of a filtration is the Postnikov filtration on a spectrum
X
, which is assembled using the covers functors in the t-structure on the category of spectra:

\tau_{\geq\bullet}X=(\cdots\to\tau_{\geq n+1}X\to\tau_{\geq n}X\to\tau_{\geq n-1}X\to\cdots)\,.

The speeded up version

\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}X=(\cdots\to\tau_{\geq 2n+2}X\to\tau_{\geq 2n}X\to\tau_{\geq 2n-2}X\to\cdots)

is referred to as the
double–speed Postnikov filtration
.
Note that what we call the Postnikov filtration here is sometimes called the Whitehead filtration in other places to remind the reader that we are using the covers rather than the truncations.
However, as the rest of the community seems intent on using the terminology Postnikov filtration for both, we surrender to the peer-pressure.

We will also consider the integers as a discrete space, which we will denote as
\mathbb{Z}^{\delta}
to distinguish it from
\mathbb{Z}
with its poset structure.
The
\infty
-category of
graded spectra
is simply the functor category

\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}=\Fun(\mathbb{Z}^{\delta},\Sp)\,.

Some constructions and notations on this category:

•

If
X
is a graded spectrum, then we denote the object in weight
p
by
X^{p}
.
Consider the functor
\mathrm{ev}_{p}:\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}\to\Sp
which picks out the
p
th weight part of our graded spectrum.
This admits a fully faithful left adjoint
\mathrm{ins}_{p}:\Sp\to\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}
.
On objects it is given by

(\mathrm{ins}_{p}(A))^{n}=\begin{cases}A&\text{if $n=p$}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

The functor
\mathrm{ins}_{p}
is also right adjoint to
\mathrm{ev}_{p}
.
Often, if it is implicitly clear that we are working in the graded setting, we will simply write
A
for the graded object
\mathrm{ins}_{0}(A)
.

•

The
\infty
-category
\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}
is symmetric monoidal with the Day convolution tensor product. This is given by the formula

(X\otimes Y)^{n}\simeq\bigoplus_{i+j=n}X^{i}\otimes Y^{j}\,.

The unit of the symmetric monoidal structure is simply
\mathbb{S}
concentrated in degree
0
. That is, the unit is
\mathrm{ins}_{0}(\mathbb{S})
.

•

The inclusion
\mathbb{Z}^{\delta}\to\mathbb{Z}
induces a restriction functor
\mathrm{Res}:\Filt(\Sp)\to\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}
which simply forgets the structure maps of the filtration.
This functor admits a left adjoint
\mathrm{spl}:\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}\to\Filt(\Sp)
which on objects are given by the formula

\mathrm{spl}(X)^{n}=\bigoplus_{i\geq n}X^{i}\,.

•

We will write

\gr^{q}(X)=X^{q}/X^{q+1}

and call this the
q
th associated graded
.
Together, these assemble into a
total associated graded
functor

\gr:\Filt(\Sp)\longrightarrow\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}\,,\quad X\mapsto(\gr^{q}X)_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}\,.

This functor is symmetric monoidal.
Moreover, maps between complete filtrations are equivalences if they are equivalences on the total associated graded.

•

We will write
(k):\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}\to\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}
to denote the functor that shifts a graded object
k
times:

(X(k))^{n}=X^{n-k}\,.

For example,
X=\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[1](1)
is the graded spectrum that is given as

X^{n}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{Z}&\text{for $n=0$}\\
\mathbb{Z}[1]&\text{for $n=1$}\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

1.2.
The even filtration

For us, an
even ring spectrum
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
with the property that homotopy groups in odd degrees vanish.
We follow
[
10
]
*Definition 1.1.1, and define for any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
, the filtration

\fil_{\mathrm{ev}}^{n}(A)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}A\to R\\
R\text{ even}\end{subarray}}\tau_{\geq 2n}R\,.

This is a filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring that we will refer to as the
even filtration
on
A
.
In particular, the even filtration is the right Kan extension of the double-speed Postnikov filtration functor
\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{ev}}\to\CAlg(\Filt)
along the inclusion of even ring spectra into all
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings.
The even filtration construction is hence regarded as a functor

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}:\CAlg\longrightarrow\CAlg(\Filt)\,.

Proposition 1.2
.

The following are true for the functor
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}:\CAlg\to\CAlg(\Filt)
:

(1)

The even filtration of an even ring spectrum is the double–speed Postnikov filtration.

(2)

The even filtration construction is lax symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

(1)

Follows from
A=A
being initial among all maps
A\to R
with
R
even, whenever
A
is itself even.

(2)

The two symmetric monoidal structures are cocartesian and by
[
17
]
*Proposition 2.4.3.8 any functor between categories with cocartesian symmetric monoidal structures is lax symmetric monoidal.

∎

In many cases, we can compute the even filtration via descent properties, see
[
10
]
*Section 2.2.

Definition 1.3
.

A map
A\to B
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring is
evenly faithfully flat
, or
eff
for short, if for any even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
C
and a map
A\to C
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, the pushout
B\otimes_{A}C
is itself even and the induced map
\pi_{*}(C)\to\pi_{*}(C\otimes_{A}B)
is faithfully flat.

Proposition 1.4
.

If
A\to B
is eff, then the canonical map

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(A)\to\lim_{\Delta}(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(B^{\otimes_{A}\bullet+1}))

is an equivalence.

In particular, we will use the
eff
ness of the following maps extensively in this paper.

Example 1.5
.

The map
\mathbb{S}\to\MU
is eff.
Hence, by the proposition, we have that the even filtration on the sphere spectrum is

\displaystyle\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\fil_{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}(\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\tau_{\geq 2\ast}(\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1})\right)

where we have used that tensor powers of
\MU
are even.

Proposition 1.6
.

The augmentation
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\to\mathbb{S}
is eff. In particular,
k[S^{1}]\to k
is eff for any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
k
.

Proof.

We need to check that the pushout along any map
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\to C
with
C
even is even and free over
C
.
Note that

C\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]}\mathbb{S}\simeq C[\mathbb{CP}^{\infty}]

whose homotopy groups are well-known to be even and free by an argument using the Atiyah–Hirzebruch spectral sequence.
∎

The following proposition, while not immediately important, will be crucial in making the constructions made later in the paper work out.

Proposition 1.7
.

The associated graded of the even filtration on the sphere spectrum admits a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear structure.

Proof.

Note that the even filtration is non-negatively graded, so that its total associated graded is itself non-negatively graded.
Moreover, the associated graded in filtration degree
0
is just equivalent to
\mathbb{Z}
, so that total associated graded admits a canonical
\mathbb{Z}
-action.
To see this, we can use the description of the even filtration as the totalization

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\simeq\lim_{\Delta}(\tau_{\geq 2*}(\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1}))\,.

The associated graded in filtration degree
0
will be written as a limit

\gr^{0}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\simeq\lim_{\Delta}(\pi_{0}(\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1}))\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{Z}.

∎

Remark 1.8
.

An alternative proof of the above is to use the fact that
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
is the décalage of Adams–Novikov filtration together with the equivalence

\gr^{q}\mathrm{Dec}(X)\simeq H\pi^{\mathrm{Bei}}_{q}(X)[q]

established in
[
12
]
*Theorem II.2.20, where the object on the right hand side is
\mathbb{Z}
-linear, as the heart of the Beilinson t-structure is equivalent to the category of chain complexes of abelian groups.

Remark 1.9
.

We remark that
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
is an object built out of quasi-coherent sheaves on the moduli stack of formal groups
\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{fg}}
. In particular, out of the filtration
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
, we recover the (re-indexed) Adams–Novikov spectral sequence

E^{2}_{n,2a-n}\cong\pi_{n}(\gr^{a}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))\Longrightarrow\pi_{n}(\mathbb{S})\,.

Let us furthermore remark that, since this spectral sequence is concentrated in the first quadrant, we have
\pi_{n}(\gr^{a}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))\cong 0
for
n<0
.
In particular,
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
, and by completeness,
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
will be connective in the neutral t-structure on
\Fil(\Sp)
.

1.3.
Even
\MU
-based Synthetic Spectra

By work of Gheorghe–Isaksen–Krause–Ricka, the category of modules over the even filtration of the sphere spectrum can be identified with what is known as the category of even
\MU
-based synthetic spectra
[
9
]
.
In this paper, we will simply treat this as the definition of synthetic spectra.

Definition 1.10
.

We set

\mathrm{SynSp}=\operatorname{Syn}_{\MU}^{\mathrm{ev}}=\Mod_{\fil_{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}(\mathbb{S})}(\Filt(\Sp))

to denote the stable
\infty
-category of (even
\MU
-based) synthetic spectra.
We use the notation

\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}=\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr})

to denote graded modules over the associated graded
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
of the even filtration on
\mathbb{S}
.

In what follows, we will typically drop the descriptions “even” and “
\MU
-based” and simply speak of
synthetic spectra
.
Since we will take this perspective on synthetic spectra, we allow ourselves to talk about the associated graded and the underlying object of a synthetic spectrum, without further explanation.
Note that the even filtration on any spectrum is an example of a synthetic spectrum.
Since the even filtration on the sphere spectrum is a filtered
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring, the category of synthetic spectra is a closed symmetric monoidal category.
The even filtration on the sphere spectrum is evidently the unit for the monoidal structure, and because of this we will often speak of
\fil_{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}(\mathbb{S})
as the
synthetic sphere spectrum
and denote it by

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}=\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\,.

Similar to our notation for the synthetic sphere spectrum, the even filtration on
\mathbb{Z}
will also be denoted
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}
.
Since
\mathbb{Z}
is already even, the even filtration on
\mathbb{Z}
is just equivalent to the double–speed Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}
, which is itself just the trivial filtration on
\mathbb{Z}
.
That is

\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}=\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\,.

Much of what is to follow regards what happens to certain constructions when base-changed along the
synthetic Hurewicz map

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\to\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}
.

2.
Synthetic Chains and Cochains on the Circle

In this section, we describe a canonical filtration on spherical chains and cochains on the circle, from the point of view even filtrations.
In §
2.1
, we review Postnikov filtrations on integral chains and cochains of the circle following
[
27
]
.
In §
2.2
, we note that the Postnikov filtration on the former actually coincides with its even filtration and use this observation to construct the correct filtrations on spherical chains and cochains of the circle for our purposes.

2.1.
Filtered integral chains and cochains

We begin with a brief discussion of what happens
\mathbb{Z}
-linearly.
Let us write
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}:S^{1}\to\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
for the constant functor with value
\mathbb{Z}
.
We follow notation of
[
27
]
and write

\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]=\colim_{S^{1}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}^{\vee}=\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}=\lim_{S^{1}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\,.

Both of these objects admit bicommutative bialgebra structures that are dual to one-another.
We want to study the structure of the filtered variants

\mathbb{T}_{\fil}=\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{T}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{\fil}^{\vee}=\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{T}^{\vee}\,.

Proposition 2.1

(Raksit)
.

There are unique bicommutative bialgebra structures on
\mathbb{T}_{\fil}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\fil}
that promote the bicommutative bialgebra structure on
\mathbb{T}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}
, respectively.
Note that the statement of uniqueness guarantees that the bicommutative bialgebra structure on
\mathbb{T}_{\fil}
and
\mathbb{T}_{\fil}^{\vee}
are dual.

Proof.

The Postnikov filtration functor is lax symmetric monoidal and fully faithful.
Consider the full subcategory of
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
spanned by those objects that are coproducts of suspensions of
\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular, this is a symmetric monoidal subcategory of
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
and the restriction of the functor
\tau_{\geq\bullet}(-)
to this full subcategory is (strictly) symmetric monoidal.
Since
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
is an object of this subcategory, it follows that
\mathbb{T}_{\fil}=\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{T}
admits a bicommutative bialgebra structure.
∎

2.2.
The even filtration on chains and cochains

Our goal for now is to generalize the above section, but using
\mathbb{S}
in place of
\mathbb{Z}
and the even filtration in the place of the Postnikov filtration.
This is indeed a reasonable thing to do, and a direct generalization, since the even filtration on
\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
coincides with its Postnikov filtration, as the following results show.

Lemma 2.2
.

There is a natural equivalence

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}])\simeq\tau_{\geq*}(\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}])\,.

Proof.

The result will follow from comparing the associated graded of both sides. Indeed, the Postnikov filtration on a (bounded below) spectrum
E
is the unique filtration with its associated graded, up to equivalence. The map realizing the equivalence can be built recursively in each degree, starting with the lower bound on the non-trivial homotopy of
E
.
With this in mind, recall that the total associated graded of
\tau_{\geq\ast}\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
is an exterior algebra over
\mathbb{Z}
with a generator in degree
1
and weight
1
. On the other hand, to understand the associated graded of the even filtration, note that the augmentation map
\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]\to\mathbb{Z}
is evenly faithfully flat by Proposition
1.6
.
We are thus trying to understand the limit

\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}])\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\hbox to187.85pt{\vbox to19.37pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 93.92558pt\lower-9.68251pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right)\,.

For this, note that we may identify
\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]}\mathbb{Z}
with
\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{CP}^{\infty}]
which is even.
It is well–known that

\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]}\mathbb{Z})\simeq\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{post}}(\mathbb{Z}[\mathbb{CP}^{\infty}])

is a free divided power coalgebra over
\mathbb{Z}
with a generator in degree
2
and weight
1
.
Hence, the cosimplicial diagram of graded objects we have described above is precisely the cobar construction on this graded object over
\mathbb{Z}
.
The cobar construction applied to such an object in known to be an exterior algebra over
\mathbb{Z}
with a generator in degree
1
and weight
1
.
This is exactly the associated graded of
\tau_{\geq\ast}\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
as well.
∎

Note that a similar result and proof work with
\mathbb{Z}
replaced with some other even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
k
.

Lemma 2.3
.

For any even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
k
, there is a natural equivalence

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])\simeq\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1).

Proof.

Recall that if
k
is even, then the augmentation map
k[S^{1}]\to k
is evenly faithfully flat by Proposition
1.6
.

with
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1)
.
For this, note that we may identify
k\otimes_{k[S^{1}]}k
with
k[BS^{1}]
.
Each term in the above cosimplicial diagram is then just the even filtration on an even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring, i.e. the double speed Postnikov filtration, which is complete.
Since complete filtrations are closed under limits, we conclude that
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])
must necessarily be complete, as well.

We define a map
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])\to\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1)
of complete filtered spectra which we show is an equivalence upon identifying the associated graded of the left hand side. Clearly there is a map
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])\to\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)
, so we focus our attention on the construction of a map
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])\to\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1)
. For this note that we have a map

\colim(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}]))\to k[S^{1}]\simeq k\oplus k[1]\to k[1]

of
k
-module spectra. This admits as an adjoint the map
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])\to\tau_{\geq 2*}(k[1])
. We are implicitly viewing this as an adjunction between
k
-modules and the connective part of the (double-speed) Postnikov t-structure on
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)
-modules. Note moreover that
\tau_{\geq 2*}(k[1])
is by definition equivalent to
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1)
. This gives the desired map.

Now, since equivalences of complete filtrations can be detected on the associated graded, it suffices to identify the associated graded of the cosimplicial diagram presentation of the source with the associated graded which in weight
n
is given by

\gr^{n}(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1))\simeq\pi_{2n}(k)[2n]\oplus\pi_{2n-2}(k)[2n+1]\,.

By definition, the associated graded given of the cosimplicial diagram in question is the cobar construction on the graded ring
\pi_{2*}(k[BS^{1}])
over
\pi_{2*}(k)
.
It is well–known that
\pi_{2*}(k[BS^{1}])
is a free divided power coalgebra over
\pi_{2*}(k)
with a generator in degree
2
and weight
1
.
The cobar construction applied to such an object is an exterior algebra over
\pi_{2*}(k)
with a generator in degree
1
and weight
1
which is exactly
\pi_{2*}(k)\oplus\pi_{2*}(k)[1](1)
.
∎

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])\simeq\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}[1](1)

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 114.97 -7.12 L 147.08 -7.12}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{106.49173pt}{-5.14221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 147.35 -7.12)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right)\,.

Now, Lemma
2.3
displays a natural equivalence,

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k[S^{1}])\simeq\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(k)[1](1).

for any even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
k
.
In particular, we obtain an equivalence of cosimplicial objects between the one computing
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])
and one whose terms are of the form
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\MU^{\otimes n+1})\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\MU^{\otimes n+1})[1](1)
, and which totalizes to
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[1](1)
. This equivalence therefore totalizes to an equivalence

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}[1](1)\simeq\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])\,,

as desired.
∎

We now check that the even filtration on the
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]
base-changes to the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{T}
.

Proposition 2.5
.

The even filtration on spherical chains on the circle base changes to the Postnikov filtration along the synthetic Hurewicz map in the sense that

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]\,.

Proof.

The even filtration is lax symmetric monoidal, so we certainly have a natural map

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\longrightarrow\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}])\simeq\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]\,.

Then the proposition follows from Lemma
2.3
, since, at the level of modules, the natural map then takes the form

\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}[1](1)\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\oplus\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}[1](1)\simeq\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]\,.

∎

Remark 2.6
.

We do not expect that the even filtration on spherical chains base-changes to the Postnikov filtration on integral chains in general. Hence we wonder if there is a certain characterization of an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid
X
so that
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}[X])
base-changes to the Postnikov filtration
\tau_{\geq\bullet}(\mathbb{Z}[X])
.

Having settled that the generalization that we intend to do is indeed a reasonable thing to do, we now introduce the main players of this section:

\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}=\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}=\hom_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}],\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\,.

Warning 2.7
.

Note that, and this is a crucial point, that

\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}\not\simeq\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}\,,

in stark difference to the definition
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\fil}=\tau_{\geq\bullet}(\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}})
in the integral context of Section
2.1
, where the notation
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\fil}
is also justified by the fact that the Postnikov filtration there preserves the duality of
\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
and
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
, by Proposition
2.1
.
Indeed, this failure can be seen already on the level of integral chains and cochains.
Since
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{T}\simeq\mathbb{T}_{\fil}
, we have that

(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{T})^{\vee}\simeq(\mathbb{T}_{\fil})^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\fil}\,.

However, the even filtration
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{T}^{\vee})
is equivalent to the trivial filtration on
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}
.
This can be seen by noting that the map
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}\to\mathbb{Z}
is eff, so that

\displaystyle\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z}^{\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}\bullet+1})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\bullet})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(L_{0}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\bullet})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}\,.

Proposition 2.8
.

The underlying
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
-modules of
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}
are dualizable.

Proof.

This follows directly from Lemma
2.4
where it was shown that

\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}(1)[1]

as a
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
-module. Thus it is perfect, hence dualizable, since we are working in a category of modules over a (filtered) ring spectrum.
∎

Proposition 2.9
.

We have that
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}
is a filtration on the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra
\map(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}],\mathbb{S})
.

Proof.

This statement follows from the fact that the underlying functor

\colim:\Filt(\Sp)\to\Sp

is symmetric monoidal, and so is

\colim:\Mod_{X}(\Filt(\Sp))\longrightarrow\Mod_{\colim X}(\Sp)

for any filtered ring spectrum
X
.
In particular, the functor
\colim:\Mod_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\to\Sp
is symmetric monoidal, and thus sends duals to duals.
Here, we use that the even filtration on
\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}
is exhaustive, which follows for example from the fact that
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}[1](1)\simeq\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])
.
∎

We now want to prove that
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}
come equipped with the structure of bicommutative bialgebras in synthetic spectra.
To prove this, we first need the following lemma.

Proposition 2.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the full subcategory of
\CAlg
spanned by the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras which are of the form
\mathbb{S}[(S^{1})^{n}]
for
n\geq 0
.
Then the restriction
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(-)|_{\mathcal{C}}
is strongly symmetric monoidal.

Proof.

Let
A
and
B
be spherical chains on tori.
We want to show that the natural map

(1)

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{syn}}}\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(B)\to\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(A\otimes B)

is an equivalence.
First, note that by
[
10
]
*Corollary 2.2.17, for
M
the spherical chains on some torus, we may compute the even filtration as

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(M)\simeq\lim_{\Delta}(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(M\otimes\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1}))

Now, each of the terms
M\otimes\MU^{\otimes n+1}
admits an eff map

M\otimes\MU^{\otimes n+1}\to\MU^{\otimes n+1},

which may be seen by a simple Tor spectral sequence argument.
Moreover, these are all compatible with the cosimplicial structure maps. More precisely, we have a map

M\otimes\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1}\to\MU^{\otimes\bullet+1}

of cosimplicial objects, essentially induced by taking compatible maps of spaces to the point.
Taking the conerve of this map of cosimplicial objects gives rise to a bi-cosimplicial object, on which applying the even filtration level-wise and then totalizing computes
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(M)
.
Note that in each bidegree
(n,m)
, the filtration just reduces to the double-speed Postnikov filtration of an even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra.
For example, for
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]
, the degree
(1,1)
piece will be
\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}((\MU\otimes\MU)[\mathbb{C}P^{\infty}])
.

In this way, we may expand the map (
1
) to a map of (bi)cosimplicial objects

\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(A)^{\bullet,\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{syn}}}\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(B)^{\bullet,\bullet}\to\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(A\otimes B)^{\bullet,\bullet}.

We claim that this map is levelwise on the bi-cosimplicial diagram, an equivalence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras. In each bidegree
(n,m)
this is just the lax symmetric monoidal structure map

\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}(A^{n,m})\otimes\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}(B^{n,m})\to\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}((A\otimes B)^{n,m}),

of the double speed filtration. To prove the claim, let’s for simplicity assume that
A=B=\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]
, the general case follows with the obvious modifications. In this case, in degree
(n,m)
, the map will look like

\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}((\MU^{\otimes n+1}[{BS^{1}}])^{\otimes m})\otimes\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}((\MU^{\otimes n+1}[{BS^{1}}])^{\otimes m})\to\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}((\MU^{\otimes n+1}[BS^{1}\times BS^{1}])^{\otimes m}).

Note that each of

(\MU^{\otimes n+1}[{BS^{1}}])^{\otimes m}\,\,\,\operatorname{and}\,\,\,(\MU^{\otimes n+1}[BS^{1}\times BS^{1}])^{\otimes m}

are quasi-free over
\MU^{n+1}
, in the sense that they are each of the form
\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\MU^{n+1}[2k_{i}]
as
\MU^{n+1}
-modules. We further claim that the double speed Postnikov tower

\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}:\Mod_{\MU^{n+1}}\to\Mod_{\tau_{\geq 2\bullet}(\MU^{n+1})}(\Fil(\Sp))

is strongly symmetric monoidal on quasi-free objects. Since the (double-speed) Postnikov tower is a complete filtration, it amounts to checking the equivalence on associated graded objects, which further just amounts to showing that the homotopy groups of the tensor product are the tensor product of the associated gradeds. This follows directly from
[
17
, Proposition 7.2.1.17]
, thus proving the levelwise claim.

Summarizing, we have shown that the natural lax monoidal structure map on these objects can be expanded to a map of bi-cosimplicial objects, which is a levelwise equivalence. Taking the limit of this levelwise equivalence of bicosimplicial objects now recovers the map (
1
) and displays it as an equivalence.
∎

Proposition 2.11
.

The synthetic spectra
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}
and
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}
are bicommutative bialgebras.

Proof.

By the previous proposition,
\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])=\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}
is a bicommutative bialgebra, since the symmetric monoidality guarantees that the coalgebra maps lift to the filtered level.
Furthermore, this will be a dualizable object, as it is simply a finite direct sum of invertible
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
-modules. Thus we conclude that its dual,
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}
, is itself a bicommutative bialgebra.
∎

Remark 2.12
.

Note that the equivalence of Lemma
2.4
is only true as modules, and not as algebras.
Indeed, already on the level of spectra we have the equivalence
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\simeq\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}[1]
in the category of spectra.
This equivalence is not true as algebras in spectra, though, given the relation
d^{2}=\eta d
in the homotopy groups

\pi_{*}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\cong\pi_{*}(\mathbb{S})[d]/(d^{2}=\eta d)

where
|d|=1
and
\eta
is the generator of
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{S})\cong\mathbb{Z}/2
.
However, the coalgebra structure on
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]
is the expected one; in the homotopy groups the element
d
is primitive.
See for example
[
13
]
*Proposition 3.3.
The synthetic spectrum
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}
inherits this property; the coalgebra structure is the expected one, but the algebra structure is not split.
When we dualize, the situation is reversed; the algebra structure of
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}
is the expected one, but the coalgebra structure is not obvious.

3.
Derived Algebraic Geometry and HKR

In this section, we give a recollection of various derived algebro-geometric preliminaries to our work.
In §
3.1
, we give a side by side reminder in broad strokes of derived and spectral algebraic geometry.
In §
3.2
-
3.3
, we recall some basic aspects of geometric stacks, and their categories of quasi-coherent sheaves.
In §
3.4
, we briefly review the theory of affine stacks, developed originally in
[
29
]
.
Finally, in §
3.5
, we recall the yoga of filtrations and quasi-coherent sheaves on the stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

3.1.
Derived vs. spectral algebraic geometry

We recall that there are essentially two potential candidates for what one means when one refers to a generalization of algebraic geometry to a higher categorical setting.

(1)

There is
spectral algebraic geometry
, where the affine objects are connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings.
This is the generalization of algebraic geometry that has been extensively studied by Lurie
[
19
]
.
In this paper, we will mainly work in this setting.

(2)

There is
derived algebraic geometry
, where the affine objects are animated commutative rings
4
4

4

Also called
simplicial commutative rings
.
.
We recall that the
\infty
-category of animated rings is the completion of the category smooth ring with respect to sifted colimits.
This is the setting used for example in the construction of the filtered circle in
[
22
]
.

Fix a commutative ring
R
, in the classical sense.
Then we may consider any of the following categories:

(1)

The 1-category of commutative algebras over
R
denoted by
\CAlg_{R}^{\heartsuit}
.

(2)

The
\infty
-category of connective commutative
R
-algebras denoted by
\CAlg_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
.

(3)

The
\infty
-category of animated
R
-algebras denoted
\CAlg_{R}^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Let
\mathcal{C}\in\{\CAlg_{R}^{\heartsuit},\operatorname{CAlg}^{\operatorname{cn}}_{R},\CAlg_{R}^{\mathrm{an}}\}
denote either of these categories.
One may define the notion of a stack via the functor of points perspective.
That is, a stack is a functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{S}
satisfying hyperdescent with respect to a suitable topology on
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
.
We will use the following terminology:

(1)

If
\mathcal{C}=\CAlg_{R}^{\heartsuit}
, we simply call the resulting object a
stack
.
We denote the
\infty
-category of stacks over
R
by
\mathrm{Stk}_{R}
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{C}=\CAlg_{R}^{\mathrm{cn}}
, we call the resulting object a
spectral stack
.
We denote the
\infty
-category of spectral stacks over
R
by
\mathrm{sStk}_{R}
.

(3)

If
\mathcal{C}=\CAlg_{R}^{\mathrm{an}}
, we call the resulting object a
derived stack
. We denote the
\infty
-category of derived stacks over
R
by
\mathrm{dStk}_{R}
.

The
\infty
-category of stacks
\mathrm{Stk}_{R}
embeds fully faithfully into both
\mathrm{sStk}_{R}
and
\mathrm{dStk}_{R}
, but the relationship between spectral stacks and derived stacks is a bit more subtle.

In either case, one obtains an
\infty
-topos, which is Cartesian closed, so that it makes sense to talk about internal mapping objects: given any two
X,Y\in\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S})
, one forms the mapping stack
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)
.
In various cases of interest, if the source or target is suitably representable by a derived scheme or a derived Artin stack, then this is the case for
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(X,Y)
, as well.

3.2.
The fpqc topology

We recall the fpqc topology, which we will be using in this paper.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
f:A\to B
be a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings. We will say that
f
is
faithfully flat
if it satisfies the following conditions:

•

The underlying map of classical rings
\pi_{0}(A)\to\pi_{0}(B)
is faithfully flat in the classical sense.

•

The morphism
f
induces an isomorphism of graded rings

(\pi_{0}B)\otimes_{\pi_{0}A}\pi_{*}A\cong\pi_{*}B\,.

The collection of faithfully flat maps defines a Grothendieck topology on
\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}
, cf.
[
19
, Proposition B.6.1.3]

Proposition 3.2
.

There exists a Grothendieck topology on the
\infty
-category
(\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}})^{\mathrm{op}}
which can be characterized as follows:

If
A
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra, then a sieve
\mathcal{C}\subseteq(\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}})^{\mathrm{op}}_{/A}\simeq(\CAlg_{A}^{\mathrm{cn}})^{\mathrm{op}}
is a covering if and only if it contains a finite collection of morphisms
\{A\to A_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
for which the induced map

A\to\prod_{1\leq i\leq n}A_{i}

is faithfully flat.

The above topology is known as the
fpqc topology
.
It is compatible with the classical fpqc topology on classical commutative rings.

3.3.
Geometric stacks and t-structures

Essentially all the stacks which appear in this work will be quasi-geometric stacks, whose definition we recall below.
We follow
[
19
, Section 9.1]
and remind the reader that
\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
denotes the
\infty
-category of spaces which are not necessarily small; this is needed for set-theoretical issues.

Definition 3.3
.

A
quasi-geometric stack
is a functor
X:\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
which satisfies the following conditions:

(1)

The functor
X
satisfies descent with respect to the fpqc topology.

(2)

The diagonal map
\delta:X\to X\times X
is quasi-affine.

(3)

There exists a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
and a faithfully flat morphism
f:\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to X
.

The quasi-geometric stacks which arise in this paper will be of the form expressed in the following proposition, where we refer the reader to
[
19
, Corollary 9.1.1.5]
for a proof and for definitions of the various terms which arise in the statement.

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a simplicial object of
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
satisfying the following conditions:

•

The functor
X_{0}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
is a quasi-geometric stack.

•

X_{\bullet}
is a groupoid object of
\Fun(\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}},\widehat{\mathcal{S}})
.

•

The face map
d_{0}:X_{1}\to X_{0}
is representable, quasi-affine, and faithfully flat.

Then, the geometric realization
X=|X_{\bullet}|
, formed in the
\infty
-category of fpqc sheaves, is a quasi-geometric stack.

Construction 3.5
.

Let
S
be a fixed spectral stack. Let
G\to S
be a commutative affine group scheme over
S
, namely a grouplike
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid in
\operatorname{sStk}_{S}
.
The classifying stack
BG
of
G
is given by geometric realization of the following simplicial object:

\hbox to200.04pt{\vbox to16.32pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 100.01648pt\lower-8.15973pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-100.01648pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -138.39 -3.46)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{

{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{{}}}{{{}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}

where the structure maps are the standard ones induced by the group structure on
G
.

We collect some key properties about the value of the functor
\QCoh
restricted to the class of quasi-geometric stacks in the following proposition.

Proposition 3.6
.

[
19
, Corollary 9.1.3.2]

Let
X:\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{S}}
be a quasi-geometric stack.
Then:

(1)

The stable
\infty
-category
\QCoh(X)
is presentable.

(2)

There exists a
t
-structure
(\QCoh(X)_{\geq 0},\QCoh(X)_{\leq 0})
on
\QCoh(X)
.

(3)

The
t
-structure on
\QCoh(X)
is compatible with filtered colimits, in the sense that
\QCoh(X)_{\leq 0}
is closed under filtered colimits in
\QCoh(X)
.

(4)

The
t
-structure on
\QCoh(X)
is both left and right complete.

Remark 3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{F}\in\QCoh(X)
, and let
u:\mathrm{Spec}(A)\to X
be a faithfully flat cover. Then
\mathcal{F}\in\QCoh(X)_{\geq 0}
if and only if
u^{*}(\mathcal{F}))\in(\Mod_{A})_{\geq 0}
.

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
X
be a quasi-geometric stack whose restriction as a functor to discrete commutative rings is denoted by
X^{\operatorname{cl}}
.
There is an equivalence

\QCoh(X)^{\heartsuit}\simeq\QCoh(X^{\operatorname{cl}})^{\heartsuit}\,.

We can interpret the above proposition as saying that the heart of the t-structure on
\QCoh(X)
is insensitive to the derived structure on
X
, and is precisely the standard abelian category of quasi-coherent sheaves on the classical truncation
X^{\operatorname{cl}}
.

3.4.
Affine stacks

Fix a discrete commutative ring
R
, and focus for the moment on the class of (higher) stacks; in particular our category of affine objects is discrete commutative rings. In this section, we briefly review the theory of
affine stacks
, set forth by Toën in
[
29
]
.

Definition 3.9
.

We write
\mathrm{coSCR}_{R}
for the
\infty
-category of cosimplicial commutative rings over
R
. This refers to the underlying
\infty
-category of the combinatorial model structure on cosimplicial commutative rings established in
[
29
, Théorème 2.1.2]
. We remark that as such, this category contains all small limits and colimits.

Remark 3.10
.

It is expected that this
\infty
-category is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of coconnective derived rings, in the sense of
[
27
, Section 4]
.
This is confirmed in work of Mathew–Mondal
[
20
]
.

The natural inclusion functor
\CAlg_{R}^{\heartsuit}\to\mathrm{coSCR}_{R}
gives rise to a functor
\operatorname{Aff}_{R}\to(\mathrm{coSCR}_{R})^{\mathrm{op}}
, which may be right Kan extended to the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Stk}_{R}
of stacks over
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
.
This is the functor
R\Gamma(-,\mathcal{O})
of taking global sections.
There is an adjunction

R\Gamma(-,\mathcal{O}):\operatorname{Stk}\longrightarrow\mathrm{coSCR}^{\mathrm{op}}:\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}\,,

where the right adjoint is given as

\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}(A)(B)=\Map_{\mathrm{coSCR}}(A,B)\,.

This functor is fully faithful, and we refer to its essential image as the
\infty
-category of
affine stacks
over
R
.
We denote this as
\mathrm{AffStk}_{R}
.

Definition 3.11
.

Modulo some set theoretic technicalities, we may view the
\infty
-category of affine stacks
\operatorname{AffStk}
as a localization of the category of (pre)-stacks.
In particular, any
X\in\operatorname{Stk}
that can be written as a small colimit of affine schemes possesses an initial map

X\to\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}(R\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}))

to an affine stack which is an equivalence on derived global sections.
The right hand side is often referred to as
affinization
of
X
.

Remark 3.12
.

We isolate a key phenomenon in the theory which will be important to our constructions. Suppose

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}

is a Cartesian square of affine stacks.
Then, by the equivalence of
\operatorname{AffStk}_{R}
with
\mathrm{coSCR}_{R}
, one obtains a cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(W,\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(Y,\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(Z,\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O})}

of cosimplicial commutative rings.

Example 3.13
.

Suppose that
X=BG
is a pointed connected affine stack.
Then the Cartesian diagram of affine stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BG}

implies an equivalence

R\Gamma(G,\mathcal{O})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{R\Gamma(BG,\mathcal{O})}\mathbb{Z}\,.

Remark 3.14
.

Let
S\in\operatorname{Stk}_{\mathbb{Z}}
be a fixed base stack.
One may study a notion of
relative affine stacks
over
S
.
This is studied in
[
24
]
in some depth, particularly when
S=\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Specializing to this particular case, one may study the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{coSCR}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
of cosimplicial commutative algebras over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
By the Rees equivalence, see Section
3.5
, this is the same as cosimplicial commutative algebras in
\Fil(\Mod{Z})
.
There will be a fully faithful embedding

\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}:\operatorname{coSCR}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}\to\operatorname{Stk}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}

with essential image given by the
\infty
-category of affine stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Remark
3.12
holds true when replacing
R
with
S
.

Example 3.15
.

We give a parametrized variant of Example
3.13
.
Let
X=BG
be a pointed connected affine stack over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Then there is a Cartesian diagram of affine stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BG}

which will imply an equivalence

R\Gamma(G,\mathcal{O})\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{R\Gamma(BG,\mathcal{O})}L_{0}\mathbb{Z}

of cosimplicial commutative algebras in filtered
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.
We will see in Section
5.4
that the filtered Hilbert additive group will be of this form and thus also the degree filtration on integer-valued polynomials.

Remark 3.16
.

As mentioned in the introduction, from a more conceptual point of view, one could argue that this paper is part of a larger study of trying to understand exactly what the proper definition of an affine stack in the setting of spectral algebraic geometry should be.

3.5.
The geometry of filtrations

In this section, we summarize some of the main results of
[
23
]
for the convenience of the reader.
The main players of what follows are the affine schemes:

\mathbb{A}^{1}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{N}])\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{G}_{m}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{Z}])\,.

Note that the group completions map
\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{Z}
induces a map
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
of spectral schemes.
Moreover, the natural numbers is a comodule over the integers via the coaction

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{Z}\,,\quad n\mapsto(n,n)\,,

and this induces an action

\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}

of stacks.
We will define
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
to be the quotient, in the
\infty
-category of fpqc sheaves on connective commutative algebras, with respect to this action.
See for details
[
19
]
*Appendix B.
The quotient
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is a geometric stack, so its
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves is well-behaved; in particular, it is presentable stable
\infty
-category with a symmetric monoidal structure by Proposition
3.6
.

Since
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is a group scheme, we can also consider its classifying stack
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
The second author proves the following equivalences.

Proposition 3.17

(Moulinos)
.

We have equivalences

\QCoh(B\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr}\quad\text{and}\quad\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq\Filt(\Sp)

of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

We will refer to the functor which assigns to a filtered spectrum a quasi-coherent sheaf over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
as the
Rees construction
.
In particular, we will denote the symmetric monoidal functor by

\mathrm{Rees}:\Filt(\Sp)\longrightarrow\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\,.

Geometrically, the stack
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is built out of two points:

•

There is a unique map of stacks
1:\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
which induces a functor
1^{*}:\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\to\Sp
which corresponds to the colimit functor under the Moulinos equivalence. This point is called the
generic
point.

•

There is a unique map of stacks
0:B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
which induces a functor
0^{*}:\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\to\QCoh(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
which corresponds to the total associated graded under the Moulinos equivalences. This point is called the
closed
point.

Motivated by the above, we make the following definition.

Definition 3.18
.

A
graded stack
is a stack over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
A
filtered stack
is a stack over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
If
X
is a filtered stack, then the
associated graded stack
of
X
, denoted
X^{\mathrm{gr}}
, is the base-change of
X
along the closed point.
The
underlying stack

X^{u}
is the pullback along the generic point.

Definition 3.19
.

•

We will denote the composition

\CAlg(\Filt(\Sp))\overset{\mathrm{Rees}}{\longrightarrow}\CAlg(\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}))\overset{\mathrm{Spec}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Stk}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}

by
\mathrm{fSpec}
.

•

We will denote the composition

\CAlg(\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr})\longrightarrow\CAlg(\QCoh(B\mathbb{G}_{m}))\overset{\mathrm{Spec}}{\longrightarrow}\mathrm{Stk}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}

by
\mathrm{grSpec}
.

In particular, we will be applying this construction to the synthetic sphere spectrum, and will often be working over this base.
This will fit into the following diagram of pullback squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{grSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}\,.}

4.
Formal Groups and Cartier Duality

In this section, we recall some basics on formal groups, mainly following
[
18
]
*Section 1.
In §
4.1
, we review the notion of smooth coalgebras and formal hyperplanes.
In §
4.2
, we review the definition of formal groups as abelian group objects in formal hyperplanes.
Finally in §
4.3
, we recall the duality of Cartier between affine groups and formal group schemes.
We will later use this to see that the Hilbert additive group is Cartier dual to the formal multiplicative group, even when working over the sphere.

4.1.
Coalgebras

We begin with some brief recollections on coalgebras.
We state our definitions in the generality of working over a fixed connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
.
Any coalgebras that we work with are assumed to be commutative
5
5

5

This is often called cocommutative, but since we use
[
18
]
as our primary source we follow Lurie’s terminology and call these commutative rather than cocommutative.
, and we will consistently write
coalgebra
when we mean
cocommutative coalgebra
.
Recall that the
\infty
-category of commutative coalgebras over
R
is defined as

\mathrm{cCAlg}_{R}=\CAlg(\Mod_{R}^{\mathrm{op}})^{\mathrm{op}}\,.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
C
be a coalgebra over
R
.
We will say that
C
is
smooth
if it is flat as an
R
-module
6
6

6

Recall that this means that
\pi_{0}C
is flat over
\pi_{0}R
in the classical sense and that
\pi_{n}C\cong\pi_{0}C\otimes_{\pi_{0}R}\pi_{n}R
.
and if the coalgebra
\pi_{0}C
is smooth as a coalgebra over
\pi_{0}R
.
Recall that smoothness of
\pi_{0}C
over
\pi_{0}R
means that it is isomorphic to the divided power algebra

\Gamma^{*}_{\pi_{0}R}(M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\Gamma^{n}_{\pi_{0}R}(M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}(M^{\otimes_{\pi_{0}R}n})^{\Sigma_{n}}

for some finitely generated projective
\pi_{0}R
-module
M
.
The
\infty
-category of flat coalgebras over
R
will be denoted
\mathrm{cCAlg}_{R}^{\flat}
and the
\infty
-category of smooth coalgebras over
R
is denoted
\mathrm{cCAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{sm}}
.
These are both full subcategories of
\mathrm{cCAlg}_{R}
.

Note that if
C
is a coalgebra over
R
, then the
R
-linear dual

C^{\vee}=\hom_{R}(C,R)

inherits the structure of an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra over
R
.
The passage from a coalgebra to its linear dual involves a loss of information, which can be dealt with by additionally equipping the linear dual with a topology.
In the case that
C
is smooth, Lurie shows that
C^{\vee}
can be equipped with the structure of an adic
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra by using the
nilradical topology
on
\pi_{0}C^{\vee}
.
He then shows that

(-)^{\vee}:(\mathrm{cCAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{sm}})^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\CAlg_{R}^{\mathrm{ad}}

is a fully faithful embedding, where the target denotes the
\infty
-category of adic
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras over
R
.
We will not cover this in detail and instead refer the interested reader to
[
18
]
*Section 1.3.

4.2.
Formal groups

Having covered coalgebras, we now want to give a definition of a formal group over
R
.
We first note that for every morphism
f:R\to A
of connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, the extension of scalars functor

f_{!}:\Mod_{R}\to\Mod_{A}\,,\quad M\mapsto A\otimes_{R}M

is symmetric monoidal, so if
C
is a coalgebra over
R
, then
A\otimes_{R}C
can be regarded as a coalgebra over
A
.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
C
be a flat coalgebra over
R
.
We define the
cospectrum of
C
to be the functor

\operatorname{cSpec}(C):\operatorname{CAlg}_{R}\to\mathcal{S}\,,\quad A\mapsto\operatorname{GLike}(A\otimes_{R}C)=\operatorname{Map}_{\operatorname{cCAlg}_{A}}(A,A\otimes_{R}C)\,.

The cospectrum construction on a smooth coalgebra can be compared to the formal spectrum construction on its linear dual.
Recall that if
A
is an adic
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra over
R
, then its
formal spectrum

\mathrm{Spf}(A)
denote the subfunctor of

\mathrm{Spec}(A):\CAlg_{R}\to\mathcal{S}\,,\quad B\mapsto\Map_{\CAlg_{R}}(A,B)

spanned by those maps
A\to B
for which the underlying ring homomorphism
\pi_{0}A\to\pi_{0}B
is continuous, viewing
\pi_{0}B
as equipped with the discrete topology.
If
C
is a smooth coalgebra, so that its linear dual
C^{\vee}
is an adic
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra, one has a natural equivalence
\mathrm{cSpec}(C)\simeq\mathrm{Spf}(C^{\vee})
.
This fact can be used to prove the following.

Proposition 4.3
.

(
[
18
, Proposition 1.5.9]
)
The cospectrum functor induces a fully faithful embedding

\operatorname{cSpec}:\operatorname{cCAlg}^{\flat}_{R}\to\Fun(\CAlg_{R},\mathcal{S})

of
\infty
-categories when restricted to smooth coalgebras.

Definition 4.4
.

The essential image of the fully faithful functor
\operatorname{coSpec}:\operatorname{cCAlg}^{\operatorname{sm}}_{R}\to\Fun(\CAlg_{R},\mathcal{S})
is denoted by
\operatorname{Hyp}(R)
and referred to as the
\infty
-category of
formal hyperplanes
over
R
.
By construction, the cospectrum functor hence furnishes an equivalence

\mathrm{cSpec}:\operatorname{cCAlg}^{\operatorname{sm}}_{R}\simeq\Hyp(R)

between smooth coalgebras and formal hyperplanes.

We now recall the definition of a formal group.

Definition 4.5
.

A
formal group
over
R
is a functor

\widehat{\mathbb{G}}:\CAlg_{R}\to\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{cn}}

with the property that the composition

\CAlg_{R}\xrightarrow{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{cn}}\xrightarrow{\Omega^{\infty}}\mathcal{S}

is a formal hyperplane.
We let
\mathrm{FGroup}(R)
denote the full subcategory of
\Fun(\CAlg_{R},\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{cn}})
spanned by the formal groups over
R
.

Given a formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
over
R
, we refer to the functor
X=\Omega^{\infty}\circ\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
as its underlying formal hyperplane.
By definition, we can write
X=\mathrm{cSpec}(C)
for some smooth coalgebra
C
.
We will write
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
for the linear dual of
C
regarded as an adic
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra.
We refer to
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
as the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring of functions
of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
.

Remark 4.6
.

Let
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
be a formal group.
By construction, there exists a smooth coalgebra
C
such that

\operatorname{cSpec}(C)\simeq\Omega^{\infty}\circ\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\,.

The group structure on the functor
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
, i.e. the fact that it lands in connective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, implies that
C
is in fact a bicommutative bialgebra.

Example 4.7
.

Recall the multiplicative formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
over
\mathbb{Z}
.
As a formal hyperplane, this is of the form

\mathrm{Spf}(\mathbb{Z}[[t]])
, where
\mathbb{Z}[[t]]
is endowed with comultiplication

t\mapsto x+y+xy\,.

This lifts to a formal group over
\mathbb{S}
, also unambiguously denoted as
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
, whose values on
R\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}
are given by

\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(R)=\fib(\mathbb{G}_{m}(R)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}(R^{\operatorname{red}}))

Here, the fiber is taken in connective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, and
\mathbb{G}_{m}\simeq\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}[\mathbb{Z}])
is the
strict
multiplicative group.

Example 4.8
.

Recall the additive formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
.
This is well–known to be equipped with group law
t\mapsto x+y
.
By
[
18
, Proposition 1.6.20]
, this does not lift to a formal group over
\mathbb{S}
.
Namely it is shown there that there is no formal hyperplane over
\mathbb{S}
, equipped with a unital multiplication, which base-changes via the map
\mathbb{S}\to\mathbb{Z}
to the underlying formal hyperplane of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
.
In fact, this is shown even over
\tau_{\leq 1}\mathbb{S}
.

Remark 4.9
.

For us, the fact that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
does not lift to the sphere spectrum presents obstructions to lifting the various
\mathbb{Z}
-filtrations of
[
22
,
25
]
naïvely to the sphere spectrum.

4.3.
Cartier duality

Let
R
be a commutative ring, in the classical sense.
