Cartier duality is an anti-equivalence of categories between formal groups over
R
and affine group schemes over
R
, and originally appeared in
[
7
]
.
We sketch how to informally think of this anti-equivalence using the cospectrum functor introduced in the previous section.
Let
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
be formal group over
R
.
By Remark
4.6
, we can extract a bicommutative bialgebra
H
, whose underlying coalgebra is smooth, such that
\Omega^{\infty}\circ\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\simeq\mathrm{cSpec}(H)
.
We take the perspective in this paper that Cartier duality is implemented by the assignment

\mathbf{D}:\mathrm{FGroup}(R)^{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow\mathrm{AffGrp}_{R}\,,\quad\widehat{\mathbb{G}}\mapsto\mathrm{Spec}(H)\,.

Let us unpack this from a different vantage point.
Then its algebra of functions
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is a complete adic algebra, equipped with a comultiplication making it a complete Hopf algebra.
Taking the continuous
\mathbb{Z}
-linear dual of this gives rise to a coalgebra

H_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}=\hom_{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}},\mathbb{Z}),

where
\mathbb{Z}
is endowed with the discrete topology. This coalgebra will be equivalent to
H
.
Since
\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}
is moreover equipped with a coalgebra structure, this gives rise to a Hopf algebra structure on
H_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}\simeq H
.
We will often refer to this object as the Hopf algebra of
distributions of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
.

Theorem 4.10
.

(Cartier duality)
There exists an anti-equivalence of categories between the category of formal groups and the category of affine group schemes whose underlying Hopf algebra of functions is smooth as a coalgebra.

Remark 4.11
.

Let
R
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring.
In this case, given a formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}=\operatorname{coSpec}(H)
, we still obtain a Hopf algebra structure on
H
, so it makes sense to consider
\mathrm{Spec}(H)
as the Cartier dual of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
.
However, this does not capture all the structure alone.
Indeed, recall that
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
is an abelian group object, so that it takes values in connective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.
This structure is not a priori detected by the representable functor
\mathrm{Spec}(H)
.

We now recall another perspective of Cartier duality, namely that of duality between group-valued functors, as studied in
[
16
, Chapter 3]
.
We may use this perspective to keep track of the Cartier dual
\mathbf{D}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}})
as a functor valued in connective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, and not just a functor valued in spaces, addressing the potential issue raised above.

Construction 4.12
.

The
\infty
-category
\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})
, being presentable, acquires a closed monoidal structure from the monoidal structure on
\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
In particular, for any object
X\in\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})
, there exists another object

\mathbf{D}(X)=\hom_{\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})}(X,\mathbb{G}_{m})

equipped with a map
\alpha:X\wedge\mathbf{D}(X)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
along with the following universal property: for every functor
Y\in\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})
, composition with
\alpha
induces the following homotopy equivalence:

\Map_{\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})}(Y,\mathbf{D}X)\simeq\Map_{\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})}(Y\wedge X,\mathbb{G}_{m})\,.

Remark 4.13
.

In
[
16
]
, Lurie introduces Cartier duality for group-like commutative monoid objects as opposed to abelian group objects in stacks.
In this setting, one sets
\hom_{\Fun(\CAlg,\operatorname{CMon}^{\operatorname{grp}})}(-,\GL_{1})
as opposed to
\hom_{\Fun(\CAlg,\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}{Z})}(X,\mathbb{G}_{m})
.
However, the requisite formal properties of the two categories for forming internal mapping objects are the same, and so the above holds in the setting of abelian group objects.
Moreover, when the input
X
is a
\Mod^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
-valued functor, there will be a natural factorization
X(R)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}(R)\to\GL_{1}(R)
for all
R\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}
via
[
18
, Remark 1.6.12]
, so the underlying stacks of morphisms will be equivalent.

Remark 4.14
.

It is shown in
[
16
, Proposition 3.5.6]
that if
X\simeq\mathrm{Spec}(H)
for a bialgebra
H
, then

\mathbf{D}(X)\simeq\operatorname{cSpec}(H)\,.

If moreover the underlying module of
H
is dualizable, then
\operatorname{cSpec}(H)\simeq\mathrm{Spec}(H^{\vee})
, and we can conclude that
\mathbf{D}^{2}(X)\simeq X
.
The anti-equivalence in
[
18
, Theorem 1.3.15]
of smooth coalgebras and adic
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras given by
C\mapsto C^{\vee}
implies that there is also an equivalence

\mathbf{D}^{2}(X)\simeq X\simeq\mathrm{Spec}(H)

when
H
is a bialgebra whose underlying coalgebra is smooth.
Thus we infer that
\mathbf{D}(\operatorname{cSpec}(H_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}}))\simeq\mathrm{Spec}(H_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})
for a formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
. By the previous remark, this
\mathrm{Spec}(H_{\widehat{\mathbb{G}}})
inherits an abelian group structure, as well.

5.
The Hilbert Additive Group Scheme

In this section, we recall the integral story of the Hilbert additive group scheme.
In §
5.1
, we describe how it appears and how it corresponds to the formal multiplicative group under Cartier duality.
In §
5.2
, we recall how this object can be lifted to a filtered stack
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
, following ideas from
[
22
]
.
In §
5.3
, motivated by Toën’s work in
[
30
]
, we give a new Witt vector model for
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
.
In §
5.4
, we show that the deloopings
K(\mathbb{H}_{\fil},n)
are all affine stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We use this to give an alternate description of the degree filtration on the integer-valued polynomials as the bar construction on the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
.

5.1.
The Hilbert additive group scheme

Consider the free binomial ring on one generator, denoted
\operatorname{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
.
This is simply the subring of
\mathbb{Q}[x]
consisting of those polynomial
f
such that
f(\mathbb{Z})\subset\mathbb{Z}
.
Because of this reason, we will refer to it as the
ring of integer-valued polynomials
.
This is a free
\mathbb{Z}
-module with basis being the Hilbert polynomials

{x\choose n}=\frac{x(x-1)\cdots(x-n+1)}{n!}\,.

Note that this inherits a Hopf algebra structure from the usual coproduct on
\mathbb{Q}[x]
in which
x
is primitive.

Definition 5.1
.

The
Hilbert additive group scheme
is the commutative group scheme

\mathbb{H}=\mathrm{Spec}\left(\operatorname{Int}(\mathbb{Z})\right)\,

over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.

Proposition 5.2
.

The Hilbert additive group scheme is the Cartier dual of the multiplicative formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Proof.

See for example
[
8
, Proposition C.1.2]
.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

Given that the Hilbert additive group scheme is the Cartier dual of the multiplicative formal group, we may recover
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
as the continuous dual
\hom^{\mathrm{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}[[x]],\mathbb{Z})
, where
\mathbb{Z}
is given the discrete topology and where we identify
\mathbb{Z}[[x]]
with the completion
\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]^{\wedge}_{(t-1)}
.
As described in
[
15
, Remark 2.12]
, we may identify each Hilbert polynomial
x\choose n
as a functional
\delta_{n}:\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]/(t-1)^{n+1}\to\mathbb{Z}
, which vanishes on
(t-1)^{k}
for
k<n
and for which
\delta_{n}((t-1)^{n})=1
.
Thus, we may think of the basis
\{{x\choose n}\}_{n\geq 1}
as giving a dual basis to the topological basis
\{(t-1)^{n}\}_{n\geq 0}
.

5.2.
The Hilbert additive group scheme and the filtered circle

Recall that the Hilbert additive group scheme was defined as the spectrum of the free binomial ring on one generator, as in Section
5.1
.
We now note that as
\operatorname{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
is a subring of
\mathbb{Q}[x]
, it inherits a natural filtration by the degree of the polynomial.
We will write
\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z}))
for this filtration.
The total associated graded of this filtration is the free divided power algebra on one generator:

\gr^{*}_{\deg}(\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})))\cong\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\,.

This in particular exhibits the cohomology of the Hilbert additive group as the underlying object of some filtration.
Explicitly, we will write

\mathbb{H}_{\fil}=\mathrm{fSpec}(\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})))

for this filtered stack.

Definition 5.4
.

The
filtered circle
is the filtered stack given by the classifying stack of
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
relative to
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We denote this filtered stack as
S^{1}_{\Fil}
.

Remark 5.5
.

We remark that this was defined only
p
-locally in
[
22
]
, and therefore, not in terms of the degree filtration on
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
. Instead, the filtration is displayed as a certain degeneration between the fixed points and kernel of the Frobenius on the
p
-typical Witt vector scheme
\mathbb{W}_{p}(-)
. In the following subsection, we will describe
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
in an analogous manner.

Theorem 5.6

(Moulinos–Robalo–Toën)
.

We have the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S^{1}_{\Fil})^{u}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{1}_{\Fil}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S^{1}_{\Fil})^{\gr}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}}

where the squares are pullbacks and we have

R\Gamma((S^{1}_{\Fil})^{u},\mathcal{O})\simeq\mathbb{T}^{\vee}

as a bicommutative bialgebra and

R\Gamma((S^{1}_{\Fil})^{\gr},\mathcal{O})\simeq H^{*}(S^{1},\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)\,.

Remark 5.7
.

The filtered circle is related to the topological circle
S^{1}
via affinization.
Indeed, let
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
denote the constant group scheme with value
\mathbb{Z}
.
There are canonical group morphisms
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{W}_{p}
factoring through
\mathbb{H}
, which after passing to classifying stacks gives us a morphism

S^{1}=B\mathbb{Z}\longrightarrow(S^{1}_{\Fil})^{u}=B\Fix\,.

This displays the target as the affinization of
S^{1}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})
.

Remark 5.8
.

Due to the discussion above, we can think of the filtered circle
S^{1}_{\Fil}
as a filtered stack which interpolates between the topological circle and its graded cohomology ring, corresponding to its associated graded stack.
Given a derived stack
X
, the mapping stack from the filtered circle to
X
, gives us an interpolation between Hochschild homology and (a filtered version of) cyclic homology on one side and derived de Rham cohomology of
X
on the other.
The generic fibre of the filtered circle recovers the topological circle cohomologically, and the special fibre recovers the trivial square zero extension, which morally is the cohomology of the circle viewed as a graded ring.

5.3.
Witt vector model of
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}

As mentioned in Remark
5.5
, the construction of the filtered circle (locally at the prime
p
) in Moulinos–Robalo–Toën does not go via the additive Hilbert group scheme, but via
p
-typical Witt vectors.
In this section, inspired by some of the constructions in
[
30
,
8
]
, we give a Witt vector model for
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Let
\mathbb{W}
denote the big Witt vector group scheme

\mathbb{W}:\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow\Ab\,,\quad R\mapsto\mathbb{W}(R)\,.

We refer the reader to
[
11
]
*Section 17 for more information about this object.
For now, we just remind the reader that
\mathbb{W}(R)
comes equipped with a natural action through ring homomorphisms by the multiplicative monoid of positive integers via Frobenii.
In other words, for each positive integer
n
, the group scheme
\mathbb{W}
is equipped with a Frobenius endomorphism
\mathrm{Frob}_{n}:\mathbb{W}\longrightarrow\mathbb{W}
which relate to one-another as
\mathrm{Frob}_{n}\circ\mathrm{Frob}_{m}=\mathrm{Frob}_{nm}
.
To relate this to the Hilbert additive group scheme, one notes that there is a monomorphism

\mathbb{H}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{W}

which identifies
\mathbb{H}
with the subfunctor of
\mathbb{W}
which is simultaneously fixed by all the Frobenius endomorphisms
[
30
]
*Proposition 2.2.
This allows us to understand the Hilbert additive group scheme locally at a prime
p
.
Indeed, the restriction of
\mathbb{W}
to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
decomposes as the product

\mathbb{W}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}=\prod_{p\not\mid n}\mathbb{W}_{p}

where
\mathbb{W}_{p}
denotes the group scheme of
p
-typical Witt vectors and the product is taken over all natural numbers that are not divisible by
p
.
The restriction of
\mathbb{H}
to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})
is then canonically isomorphic to the scheme
\mathsf{Fix}
:

\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\cong\mathsf{Fix}=\ker(\mathrm{Frob}_{p}-1:\mathbb{W}_{p}\to\mathbb{W}_{p})\,.

With this in mind, we recall from
[
30
, Section 3]
that there is a tower

\mathbb{H}\subset\cdots\subset\mathbb{H}_{k}\subset\cdots\subset\mathbb{H}_{2}\subset\mathbb{H}_{1}\subset\mathbb{H}_{0}=\mathbb{W}

where

\mathbb{H}_{k}=\bigcap_{i\leq k}\ker(\mathbb{W}\overset{\mathrm{Frob}_{p_{i}}-1}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{W})

and
p_{i}
denotes the
i
th prime number. Then

\mathbb{H}=\lim\mathbb{H}_{k},

where the limit is taken in abelian group valued sheaves in the fpqc topology.
Inspired by the above, let us study the case of the filtered Hilbert additive group scheme.

Construction 5.9
.

There is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on the ring scheme of big Witt vectors, given by multiplication via the Teichmüller representative
[-]:A\to\mathbb{W}(A)
.
Thus, for every positive integer
n
we may form the map

\mathfrak{F}_{n}=\operatorname{Frob}_{n}-[t]^{n-1}\mathrm{id}:\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\,,

such that for every
A\in\CAlg^{\heartsuit}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, it is given by

(f,t)\mapsto(\operatorname{Frob}_{n}(f)-[t]^{n-1}\cdot f,f)\,.

This map may be made
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant by endowing the source with the diagonal
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action and the target by another diagonal action, but where the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on the factor
\mathbb{W}
is twisted by the character
(-)^{n}:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Definition 5.10
.

We define

\mathbb{H}^{t}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

to be the subfunctor of all simultaneous fix points of
\mathfrak{F}_{n}
.
As each
\mathfrak{F}_{n}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, the subfunctor
\mathbb{H}^{t}
also admits a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action such that the inclusion into
\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}
is equivariant.
We set

\mathbb{H}^{t}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

to be the quotient over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We remark that this object admits an commutative group structure over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Construction 5.11
.

We give another description of
\mathbb{H}^{t}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, akin to the aforementioned description of
\mathbb{H}
in
[
30
]
as the limit of a certain tower of group schemes.
Again
p_{i}
denotes the
i
th prime number.
We recursively define a decreasing tower

\mathbb{H}^{t}\subset\cdots\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{1}\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{0}=\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\,,

of group-valued sheaves, where for each
p_{k}
we define

\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}=\ker\left(\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}:\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\to\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\right)\,.

Alternatively, each
\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}
may be thought of as the kernel of all
\mathfrak{F}_{p_{i}}
for all primes
p_{i}
for
i\leq k
. By construction then, we have an equivalence

\mathbb{H}^{t}\simeq\lim_{i}\mathbb{H}^{t}_{i}\,,

where the limit is taken in fpqc sheaves over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Everything in sight is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, so this descends to a limit description of
\mathbb{H}^{t}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
as well.

Remark 5.12
.

We claim that
\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}}\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}
is an fpqc cover, so that there is an exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}\to\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}}\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\to 0.

For this we apply Lemma
5.14
below, which shows that we can check this, via
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariance, by pulling back to the generic and special fiber of the associated map over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
By
[
30
]
, this is an fpqc cover over the generic fiber (i.e. when
t=1
). When
t=0
it is also not hard to see, since each Frobenius on the big Witt vectors is faithfully flat.
This can be seen, for example, by then applying
[
30
, Lemme 3.2]
to reduce to the
p
-typical case.
It follows that
\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}
is surjective, and so, the above sequence is indeed exact.

Proposition 5.13
.

There is an equivalence

\mathbb{H}^{\fil}\simeq\mathbb{H}^{t}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

of commutative group schemes over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Proof.

First, we let
\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{t}
be a deformation of
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
along
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
, defined to be the subring of
\mathbb{Q}[x,t]
, generated over
\mathbb{Z}[t]
by the polynomials

\frac{x(x-t)\cdots(x-t(n-1))}{n!}\,,\quad n\geq 0\,.

It is shown in
[
8
, Section D.2.5]
that this is precisely the Cartier dual of the formal group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, with group law given by
x+y+txy
, and as such it acquires a unique bialgebra structure over
\mathbb{Z}[t]
.
Moreover, it is shown in loc. cit. that there is an isomorphism

\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{t}\cong\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}h^{-n}\fil^{n}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))\,;

by giving
t
the relevant grading, this identifies the bialgebra
\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{t}
with the Rees construction applied to the degree filtration.
In the language of
[
25
]
, the group object
\mathrm{Spec}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})^{t})/\mathbb{G}_{m}
will be the Cartier dual of a filtered formal group over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, which gives a degeneration from the formal multiplication group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
to the formal additive group
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
.
Furthermore, by
[
25
, Theorem 1.4, Corollary 6.1]
, such an object is uniquely characterized as arising from the
I
-adic filtration
\mathbb{Z}[[t]]
, the coordinate ring of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
.
As the group
\mathbb{H}^{t}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
also corresponds to a degeneration between
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{a}}
, there must then exist an equivalence
\mathbb{H}^{\fil}\simeq\mathbb{H}^{t}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
∎

5.4.
Affineness of
K(\mathbb{H}_{\fil},n)

In this section, we show that
K(\mathbb{H}_{\fil},n)
is an affine stack. This is shown
p
-locally in
[
22
]
.
For the sake of completeness, we prove the general statement over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
here.
We follow very closely the strategy used in
[
30
]
to prove that
K(\mathbb{H},n)
is affine.
First, we state the following lemma, which we will use for the proof, involving a criterion for checking flatness.
This is a modification of
[
30
, Lemme 3.2]
.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
X\to Y
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, which is representable by relatively affine schemes, flat over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Then it is flat (resp. faithfully flat) if its base-change to the generic and special fiber over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
is flat (resp. faithfully flat).

Proof.

It amounts to checking this after pulling back along the flat cover
\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The hypothesis ensures that we have a map
A\to B
of commutative algebra over
\mathbb{Z}[t]
. Let
M
be an
A
-module; we would like to show that the
derived
tensor product
N=M\otimes_{A}B
is concentrated in degree zero.
Noting that
\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}[t]
and using the hypothesis of flatness over the generic fiber, we see that

N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]=(M\otimes_{A}B)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}\mathbb{Z}[t,t^{-1}]

is concentrated in degree zero.
Thus
N
is necessarily
t
-torsion in non-zero degree.
Now, if we base-change
N
along
\mathbb{Z}[t]\xrightarrow{t\mapsto 0}\mathbb{Z}
and use flatness of the map after base-changing to the special fiber, together with flat base-change, we see that
N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}\mathbb{Z}
is concentrated in degrees
[0,1]
, from which we may conclude that
N
itself is concentrated in these degrees.
Let us suppose by contradiction that
\pi_{1}(N)\nsimeq 0
, so that there exists a
t
-torsion element there.
Now, note that
\pi_{2}(N\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[t])
contains
\operatorname{Tor}_{1}^{\mathbb{Z}[t]}(\pi_{1}(N),\mathbb{Z})
as a direct summand.
Thus, it is itself non-trivial, which gives rise to a contradiction.
Thus,
\pi_{1}(N)
vanishes, for all
N
and so we can conclude that the map
f
is flat.
Faithful flatness follows similarly, as the property of being an epimorphism may be checked fiber-wise.
∎

Proposition 5.15
.

The stacks
K(\mathbb{H}_{\fil},n)
are affine stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

We first prove the following lemma involving the tower of group schemes

\mathbb{H}^{t}\subset\cdots\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{1}\subset\mathbb{H}^{t}_{0}=\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}

introduced in Construction
5.11
.

Lemma 5.16
.

For all
n\geq 1
, the natural map

K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)\to\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k},n)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Since
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)
is an Eilenberg-MacLane object, it is uniquely characterized by the fact that

\pi_{n}(K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n))\simeq\mathbb{H}^{t}

and that all other homotopy sheaves vanish.
Thus, we need to show that the same is true for
\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k},n)
, i.e. that

\pi_{n}(\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil},n))\simeq\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil}

and that
\pi_{i}(\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil},n))
vanishes otherwise.
As these homotopy sheaves will be related to
\lim^{i}
terms of the tower of the
\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}
’s, it amounts to show that these vanish.
By
[
30
, Appendice B]
, all higher
\lim^{i}
for
i>1
terms vanish; this is a consequence of the fact that taking such
\lim^{i}
’s commutes with fpqc sheafification, so this can be verified at the level of presheaves where they are seen to vanish.
Thus the only non-trivial homotopy sheaves will be

\pi_{n}(\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil},n))\simeq\mathbb{H}^{\fil}\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{n-1}(\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil},n))\simeq{\lim_{k}}^{1}\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k},

so we need only prove that
{\lim_{k}}^{1}\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}\simeq 0
.

For this, we let
\mathbb{N}^{+}
be the partially ordered set of positive natural numbers, but with the reverse ordering. We will focus on the following three
\mathbb{N}^{+}
-indexed diagrams

k\mapsto\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}\,,\quad k\mapsto\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\,,\quad k\mapsto(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k},

where we emphasize that the middle assignment is just the constant diagram. We have the following short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{*}\to\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{*}\to 0

Taking limits now gives the following exact sequence

(2)

\mathbb{H}^{\fil}=\lim_{k}\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}\to\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}\to{\lim_{k}}^{1}\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}\to 0

We will be done if we can show that the map
\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}
is an fpqc epimorphism over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
For this, we would like to apply Lemma
5.14
.
First, we claim that
\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}
is representable by a flat affine scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, which follows from Remark
5.12
.
Indeed, for each
k
we have the short exact sequence

0\to\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}\to\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}}\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}\to 0

where
\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}
is an fpqc cover, meaning that each
\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1}/\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k}
will be representable by a flat affine group scheme.
By argument via recurrence, we conclude that
\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}
is flat affine.
Thus, we have a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map

\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k},

between flat affine schemes over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Applying Lemma
5.14
, it amounts to checking this in the cases when
t=0
and when
t=1
, by way of transitivity of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action over all other fibers.
When
t=1
, this follows from the argumentation at the end of the proof of
[
30
, Lemme 3.3]
.
Let us argue about the
t=0
case.
For this, we also argue similarly, noting that by
[
30
, Lemme 3.2]
, it is enough to base-change to all fields and verify that it is a fpqc cover there.
In this case, recall first that when
p=p_{k}
and
n
are coprime, for any
p
-local ring
A
, that the
n
th Frobenius acts on
\mathbb{W}|_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(A)\simeq\prod_{n:p\nshortmid n}\mathbb{W}_{p}(A)
just by shifting; that is
\operatorname{Frob}_{n}(a_{*})_{i}=a_{ni}
.
This allows us to conclude that
\mathbb{H}_{i}^{t}|_{t=0}\simeq\mathbb{W}_{p}
for all
i<k
, and that
\mathbb{H}_{i}^{t}|_{t=0}\simeq\mathsf{Ker}
for all
i\geq k
.
This implies that the limit
(\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k})|_{t=0}
is eventually constant, and it is just
\mathbb{W}/\mathsf{Ker}
.
Thus we get the projection map

\mathbb{W}\to\mathbb{W}/\mathsf{Ker}

which is clearly an fpqc epimorphism and thus a faithfully flat map.
Thus, we conclude the desired claim for
t=0
.
To summarize, we have verified that the conditions of Lemma
5.14
hold for the map
\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}\to\lim_{k}(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1})/\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}
in order for us to conclude that it is faithfully flat.
Thus looking at the sequence
2
, we conclude that
{\lim_{k}}^{1}\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}
vanishes.
Thus, we obtain an equivalence

\pi_{n-1}(\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil},n))\simeq{\lim_{k}}^{1}\mathbb{H}^{\fil}_{k}\simeq 0,

and so, the only non-zero homotopy sheaf of the limit
\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}_{k}^{\fil},n)
is in
\pi_{n}
, so we conclude the desired result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.15
.

Recall from Proposition
5.13
that there is an equivalence

K(\mathbb{H}_{\fil},n)\simeq K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)/\mathbb{G}_{m}

We claim therefore that it is enough to show that
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)
is affine over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.
Indeed, suppose that this is the case; then there exists some
A\in\mathrm{coSCR}_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}
for which
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)\simeq\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}(A)
.
The fact that
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)
descends to an object
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)/\mathbb{G}_{m}
means that there exists a canonical grading on the cohomology, i.e. on
A\in\mathrm{coSCR}_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}
, compatible with its
\mathbb{Z}[t]
-algebra structure, for which
t
has weight
1
.
In other words, via the Rees equivalence,
A
corresponds to a cosimplicial commutative algebra in
\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq\Filt(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
.

Hence we show that
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)
is affine. For this, recall from Remark
5.12
that there is a fiber sequence

K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k},n)\to K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1},n)\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{F}_{p_{k}}}K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k-1},n).

Using that
K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{0},n)=K(\mathbb{W}\times\mathbb{A}^{1},n)
is affine, and the fact that affine stacks are closed under taking fibers, we conclude inductively that each
K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k},n)
is affine. Finally, since

K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)\to\lim_{k}K(\mathbb{H}^{t}_{k},n)

by the lemma above, we conclude that
K(\mathbb{H}^{t},n)
, using again the fact that affine stacks are closed under all (small) limits.
∎

Using the fact that
B\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
is affine over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we now give an alternative description of the degree filtration
\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))
as a relative tensor product in filtered
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

Proposition 5.17
.

(1)

There is an equivalence of bicommutative bialgebras

\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}}\mathbb{Z}

in the category
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

(2)

There is an equivalence of bicommutative bialgebras

\fil^{*}_{\deg}\Int(\mathbb{Z})\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\fil}}L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\,.

in the category
\Fil(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
.

Proof.

The first statement is already known
[
30
]
*Corollaire 3.5, so the only thing we need to prove is the second statement.
The filtered circle
S^{1}_{\fil}
of
[
22
]
may be defined as the classifying stack
B\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
By Proposition
5.15
this is an affine stack over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, given by

B\mathbb{H}\simeq\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}(\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\fil})=\mathrm{Spec}^{\Delta}(\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}})\,,

whose structure over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
can equivalently be seen as arising from the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
.
Now, by
[
24
]
*Section 4, there is an anti-equivalence of affine stacks over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
and cosimplicial commutative rings over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where we note that the latter are equivalently derived commutative rings in
\Filt(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})
. As a result, the Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}_{\fil}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{1}_{\fil}\,}

corresponds to the cocartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{0}\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))\,.}

in
\operatorname{DAlg}^{\operatorname{ccn}}_{\Filt(\Mod{Z})}
.
Thus we may describe the degree filtration on integer-valued polynomials as the bar construction applied to the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
, so that

\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\,.

∎

6.
The Spherical Hilbert Group Scheme

The main purpose of this section is to introduce a spherical lift of the Hilbert group scheme, without the filtration structure.
We present enough of the necessary ingredients to make sense of this object, and tie it in with what we have discussed so far.
In particular, in §
6.1
, we discuss the spherical Witt vectors.
In §
6.2
, we glue together spherical Witt vectors for all primes to produce a spherical lift of the integer-valued polynomials which is then used to produce the spherical Hilbert additive group scheme.
In §
6.3
, we prove that the Cartier duality between the Hilbert additive group and the formal multiplicative group lifts to the sphere spectrum.
Lastly, in §
6.4
, we give a bar construction description of the spherical lift of
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
.

6.1.
The Spherical Witt Vectors

Let
R
be a perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra.
The spherical Witt vectors
\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(R)
gives a canonical way of lifting the Witt vector construction to the spectral setting
[
18
,
5
,
2
]
.
This provides us with
spherical Witt vectors
functor

\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\to\CAlg_{\mathbb{S}_{p}}^{\operatorname{cn},\wedge}

from the category of perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras to the category of connective
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras.
The following result lists some of its important properties that we will use.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
k
be a perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra. Then
\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(k)
is
p
-completely flat over
\mathbb{S}^{\wedge}_{p}
and there are natural equivalences

\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}\simeq k\quad\text{and}\quad(\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\mathbb{W}_{p}(k)\,.

Moreover, the spherical Witt vector functor is fully faithful. The essential image of
\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}
consists of those
R\in\CAlg(\Sp^{\wedge}_{p})
which are connective and such that
\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes R
is a discrete perfect ring.

Remark 6.2
.

Given a discrete
k\in\CAlg^{\operatorname{perf}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}
, the spherical Witt vectors
\mathbb{SW}(k)
will be the unique lift of
k
to a
p
-complete algebra over the
p
-complete sphere spectrum.
This is not surprising given the perfectness of
k
, but
\mathbb{SW}(-)
packages this in a functorial way.

6.2.
Spherical lifts of perfect algebras

We will now lift the additive Hilbert group scheme to the setting of spectral algebraic geometry.
To do this, we will make use the spherical Witt vectors that we introduced in the previous section.
Note that
\operatorname{Int}(\mathbb{Z})
is an example of a
perfect commutative ring
in the sense that
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
is perfect over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
for all primes
p
.

Proposition 6.3
.

The algebra
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
is perfect over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
for all primes
p
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
is a binomial algebra, and hence
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
is
p
-Boolean in the sense of
[
2
]
*Definition 4.6.
∎

Since
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
is perfect, we can apply the spherical Witt vectors functor to it.
Note that by
[
18
]
*Remark 5.2.2, this lift is unique in the way it sits in the pushout diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{SW}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{F}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p\,.}

Now, we assemble these together for all primes to form the spherical lift of
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
.

Definition 6.4
.

We let
\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})}
denote the pullback in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p}\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(\operatorname{Int}(\mathbb{Z})/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p}\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{p}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}}

We call this the
spherical integer-valued polynomials
.

Remark 6.5
.

Note that the pullback square above fits into the larger diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Int(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathbb{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p}\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(\operatorname{Int}(\mathbb{Z})/p)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\prod_{p}\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Int(\mathbb{Z})_{\mathbb{A}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}}

where the outer rectangle is the arithmetic fracture square.
The subscript appearing in the lower right corner is the ring of adèles over
\mathbb{Q}
, given by

\mathbb{A}=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\left(\mathbb{R}\times\prod_{p}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\,.

Proposition 6.6
.

The spherical integer–valued polynomials provides a flat lift of the integer–valued polynomials in the sense that

\pi_{i}\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})}\cong\mathrm{Int}(\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\pi_{i}\mathbb{S}

for all
i\geq 0
.

In a larger generality, one has a functor

\mathbb{S}_{(-)}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\longrightarrow\CAlg(\Sp)

which assigns an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring to a perfect commutative ring, precisely in the same way as above.
This functor is fully faithful and admits the structure of a symmetric monoidal functor per
[
2
]
*Proposition 7.3.
This means that the bicommutative bialgebra structure on
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
lifts to a bicommutative bialgebra structure on
\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
.
Hence, the following definition does indeed determine an affine group scheme over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
.

Definition 6.7
.

The
spherical Hilbert affine group scheme
is defined as

\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}=\mathrm{Spec}\left(\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\,.

6.3.
Duality with the formal multiplicative group

One can note that the spherical lift of
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
is a smooth coalgebra in the sense of
[
18
]
*Definition 1.2.4.
Hence, it determines a formal hyperplane which supports a formal group structure.
We want identify this formal group with
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
.
For this, we will need the following.

Proposition 6.8
.

The Cartier dual of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
lifts uniquely to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
.
That is, if
H
is any bicommutative bialgebra such that

H\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\Int(\mathbb{Z})

then
H\simeq\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
.

Proof.

Recall that the functor
\mathbb{S}_{(-)}:\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{perf}}\to\CAlg(\Sp)
is fully faithful.
We show that
H
lies in the essential image of this functor.
For this, note that by hypothesis,

H\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}\simeq\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p

and hence that

H^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\mathbb{S}\mathbb{W}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p)\,,

which follows from relative perfectness of
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Moreover, we have

H\otimes\mathbb{Q}\simeq(H\otimes\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathbb{Q}\simeq\Int(\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\simeq\mathbb{Q}[x]\,;

we can now assemble
H
via a fracture square.
This is exactly the definition of the
\mathbb{S}_{(-)}
, so we conclude that
H
is in the essential image of the functor
\mathbb{S}_{(-)}
, and moreover that
H\simeq\mathbb{S}_{(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))}
.
We remark that since the functor is symmetric monoidal, this also accounts for the coalgebra structure on
H
, so we obtain uniqueness as a bicommutative bialgebra.
∎

As a consequence of Proposition
6.8
, the Cartier duality between
\mathbb{H}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
lifts to spectral algebraic geometry, and so we may unambiguously refer to
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
as the Cartier dual of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
.
We will exploit this duality between
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
in the following construction.

Construction 6.9
.

Let

\iota:\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}

be the inclusion of the spectral formal multiplicative group into the spectral multiplicative group.
Note that this is a map of abelian group objects in stacks; this essentially follows from the definition

\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(R)\simeq\fib(\mathbb{G}_{m}(R)\to\mathbb{G}_{m}(R^{\operatorname{red}}))

where the fiber is taken in connective
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.
We apply Cartier duality as described in §
4.3
to this group map to obtain a map

\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\to\mathbf{D}(\widehat{\mathbb{G}_{m}})\simeq\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}.

We refer to this as the Cartier duality map.

Remark 6.10
.

We note that the equivalence
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\mathbf{D}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
above follows from the fact that for any
R\in\CAlg
, there is an equivalence

\Map_{\CAlg_{R}}(R[\mathbb{Z}],R[\mathbb{Z}])\simeq\hom_{\operatorname{Ab}}(\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\,.

This is guaranteed by
[
6
, Theorem 1.1]
.
Furthermore, this can be promoted to an equivalence between
bialgebra
maps and all abelian group maps, which follows from the fact that every map of abelian groups
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}
will be a map of coalgebras, and that the coalgebra structure on
R[\mathbb{Z}]
is determined, via suspension, from the one on
\mathbb{Z}
.
Hence every such
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra map
R[\mathbb{Z}]\to R[\mathbb{Z}]
will also be a coalgebra map.

6.4.
Cochain description of the spherical integer-valued polynomials

Recall the equivalence

\Int(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}}\mathbb{Z}

asserted in Proposition
5.17
.
We will now show that this identification lifts to the sphere.
This will give an alternative description of the ring of functions on the spherical Hilbert additive group scheme, which will be helpful when endowing this with a filtration later on.

Proposition 6.11
.

There is an equivalence

\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\simeq\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\mathbb{S}

of bicommutative bialgebras.

Proof.

The extension of scalars functor
(-)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}:\Sp\to\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}

is symmetric monoidal and colimit-preserving, so that the induced functor
(-)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}:\CAlg\to\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z}}
is colimit-preserving.
The symmetric monoidal structure on
\CAlg
is cocartesian, so that the tenor product
\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\mathbb{S}
fits into the pushout

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\mathbb{S}\,.}

Since the extension of scalars functor is colimit-preserving, the diagram we get after base-changing is still a pushout square in
\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
We note that

\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}

since
S^{1}
is a finite space.
Combining this with by Proposition
5.17
, we conclude that there are equivalences

(\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\mathbb{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\Int(\mathbb{Z})\,.

We may now apply Proposition
6.8
to obtain the desired equivalence with
\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
. As we saw in the proof of Proposition
6.8
, the bialgebra structure will be uniquely determined, so that this is indeed an equivalence of bicommutative bialgebras.
∎

7.
The Synthetic Hilbert Additive Group Scheme

In this section, we introduce the eponym for this paper, the synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme.
This is the generalization of the filtered Hilbert additive groups scheme to the setting of derived algebraic geometry over synthetic spectra.
The desiderata for such an object is as follows:

(1)

It gives a lift to the synthetic sphere spectrum of the filtered Hilbert additive group scheme in the sense that it lives over
\mathrm{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
, which itself lives over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

(2)

The filtration arising from the
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
-module structure on its global sections is an spectral lift of the degree filtration
\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))
on the integer-valued polynomials.

In §
7.1
, we give a lift of the integer-valued polynomials to the setting of synthetic spectra, inspired by the bar construction description showed above. We also show that this lift is the correct one in the sense that it base-changes to the degree filtration
\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))
.
In §
7.2
, we introduce the synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme.
In §
7.3
, we investigate the special fiber of the synthetic Hilbert additive group and connect it to the kernel of the Frobenius on the p-typical Witt vectors, studied previously in
[
22
]
.

7.1.
The synthetic integer-valued polynomials

Recall that in Section
2
, we introduced a filtration on spherical cochains
\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}
using the even filtration.
More precisely, we studied the two dual bicommutative bialgebras

\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}=\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}=\hom_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}(\fil^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}\mathbb{S}[S^{1}],\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})

over the synthetic sphere spectrum.
Moreover, we saw that these filtrations base-change along the synthetic Hurewicz maps
to the Postnikov filtrations on
\mathbb{Z}[S^{1}]
and
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
, respectively, per Proposition
2.5
.
Inspired by the second statement of Proposition
5.17
, we hence make the following definition.

Definition 7.1
.

We define the
synthetic integer-valued polynomials
to be the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}=\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}

in
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
-modules.

Our desire is of course that this is indeed the sought after spectral lift of the degree filtration.
We will ultimately prove this at the end of this section.
Before that, we remark that the synthetic integer-valued polynomials inherits a bicommutative bialgebra structure, as it is bar construction applied to the augmented bicommutative bialgebra
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}
, by definition.

Remark 7.2
.

Since
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in
\Filt(\Sp)
with
\colim(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\simeq\mathbb{S}
, there is an induced symmetric monoidal functor

\colim:\mathrm{SynSp}\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\to\Mod_{\colim(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}(\Sp)\simeq\Sp.

Hence, we obtain an equivalence

\colim\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\simeq\colim\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\right)\simeq\mathbb{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\mathbb{S},

thus recovering the spherical ring of integer-valued polynomials.

We show that this is a “lift” of the degree filtration on the integer-valued polynomials.

Proposition 7.3
.

There is an equivalence

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))

of filtered objects in
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.
Here, the right hand side denotes the ring of integer-valued polynomials, equipped with its degree filtration.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.17
we have an identification

\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\,.

From this, one concludes, via symmetric monoidality of the extension of scalars functor
(-)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}
, an equivalence

\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes_{\mathbb{T}^{\vee}_{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}}\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}L_{0}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\fil^{*}_{\deg}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))\,.

∎

Remark 7.4
.

Using the machinery of
[
2
]
one can construct another filtration on
\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
by using the notion of
synthetic spherical Witt vectors
.
Indeed, in
[
2
]
*Definition 2.27, Antieau defines a functor

\mathbb{SW}_{\mathrm{syn}}:\CAlg^{\mathrm{perf}}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\longrightarrow\CAlg(\SynSp^{\wedge}_{p})

by simply post-composing the spherical Witt vectors described above with the
p
-complete even filtration functor.
We could apply this to the perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
.
However, this does not produce the same filtration on
\mathbb{SW}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p)
that we we obtain by
p
-completing
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
.
One can see this by how the two filtrations base-change over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
The filtration constructed using the synthetic spherical Witt vectors base-changes to the trivial filtration on
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
:

\mathbb{SW}_{\mathrm{syn}}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{p}}\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq L_{0}(\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p)\,.

We compare this to our construction which instead base-changes to the degree filtration on
\Int(\mathbb{Z})/p
.
Relatedly, one obtains a filtration on the spherical cochains of
S^{1}
, at least up to
p
-completion, and this will also base-change to the trivial filtration if one constructed it via synthetic Witt vectors.

7.2.
The synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme

We now introduce the synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme, an avatar in the realm of geometry of the constructions in Section
7.1
.

Definition 7.5
.

The
synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme
is

\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}:=\mathrm{fSpec}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\,.

This acquires a group structure from the bicommutative bialgebra structure on
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}
.

Remark 7.6
.

The synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme fits into the following diagram of pullback squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{grSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}\,.}

In particular, we recover the spherical Hilbert additive group as the fiber of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
.

Remark 7.7
.

By Proposition
7.3
, the synthetic Hilbert additive groups scheme base-changes along the map

\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}})\simeq\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})

to the filtered group scheme
\mathbb{H}_{\fil}
.

7.3.
The special fiber of the synthetic Hilbert additive group

We now study in closer detail the special fiber of the synthetic Hilbert additive group scheme.
The main upshot is that the associated graded stack
\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}
, which is a stack over
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
, arises as the base change of the scheme
\mathsf{Ker}
over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
. However, this does not account for the group structure, which is twisted by the relation
\eta d=d^{2}
in
\pi_{*}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])
. Hence, while the Cartier dual of
\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}
is a lift of the additive formal group to
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
, it is not the additive formal group itself.

Lemma 7.8
.

There is an equivalence

\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)\simeq\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})

of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras in
\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}
.

Proof.

We already know that such an equivalence holds at the level of modules.
Let
\delta\in\pi_{-1}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1))
be the generator in homotopy of the graded spectrum
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)
. By connectivity of
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
, this will square to zero. Thus we may define an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra map

\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)\to\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})

in graded
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, as the map
\mathbb{Z}\to\gr^{0}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
, the inclusion of the weight zero summand, together with its shift
\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)\to\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)
. Tensoring up to
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
gives, by adjunction, a
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
-linear algebra map

\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)\to\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee});

whose underlying map of modules is the equivalence referred to above. This gives the desired equivalence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras.
∎

Proposition 7.9
.

There is an equivalence

\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}\simeq\mathsf{Ker}|_{\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}

of spectral schemes over
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
where the right hand side denotes the restriction to
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
of
\mathsf{Ker}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}|{Z}
.

Proof.

Recall that

\mathsf{Ker}=\mathrm{Spec}\left(\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\right)/\mathbb{G}_{m}

where the divided power algebra
\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle
obtains its grading as the associated graded of the degree filtration on
\Int(\mathbb{Z})
.
We first define a map

\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\to\gr^{*}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)

in
\CAlg(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr})
.
For this, recall that each of the above objects admits a description as a push-out in
\CAlg(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr})
.
Indeed by Proposition
5.17
(2), there is an equivalence

\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\simeq\mathbb{Z}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)}\mathbb{Z}\,;

since pushouts are given by relative tensor products in
\CAlg(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr})
, this gives the following pushout square description in this category:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\,.}

Similarly, we have by definition an equivalence

\gr^{*}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\simeq\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\otimes_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)}\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})

and so, the following pushout diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr^{*}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\,.}

We now define a map of the two pushout diagrams. The
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra structure on
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
corresponds to a map
\mathbb{Z}\to\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
which is simply the inclusion of the weight zero component, so this gives the map on the upper right and lower left edges.
This map moreover induces a morphism of (trivial) square zero algebras

\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)\to\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\oplus\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[-1](-1).

which is by definition, compatible with the map
\mathbb{Z}\to\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
.
This gives a map of pushout diagrams and hence, upon taking colimits, a map

\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\to\gr^{*}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)\,.

Thus we have produced a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear map of graded algebras. By adjunction, this induces a
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
-linear map

\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\to\gr^{*}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right),

which will be an equivalence since pushouts commute with tensor products.
By construction, this equivalence is compatible with coalgebra structures induced on
\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle
and
\gr^{*}\left(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}\right)
from being bar constructions.
Hence, we conclude the desired equivalence of
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebras in
\CAlg(\Mod^{\gr}_{\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S})})
, which by passing to relative affine schemes, gives an equivalence

\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}\simeq\mathsf{Ker}|_{\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}

of relatively affine schemes over
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{syn}})
.
∎

As a consequence of the above proposition, the group scheme
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}
corresponding to the associated graded of the filtration on the synthetic additive group scheme fits into the following pullback square of spectral stacks over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Ker}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}\,.}

We remark that this is all made possible by the fact that
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
is
\mathbb{Z}
-linear, which is a key theme of this paper.

Warning 7.10
.

The above proposition accounts for the structure of
\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-group in spectral stacks over
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
.
This is because we have displayed its
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra of functions as a bar construction, and the proposition displayed an equivalence of bar constructions, which accounts for
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebra structures.
However, this does not account for the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-group structure on
\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.
In fact, the relation
d^{2}=\eta d
in the homotopy of
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]
manifests itself already in
\pi_{*}(\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}))
, which gives the dual
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})
, and hence its bar construction, a different
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-coalgebra structure from the expected one.

8.
The Synthetic Circle and its Further Deloopings

We have so far introduced the synthetic additive group scheme as a group stack over the evenly filtered sphere.
In this section, we will deloop this construction with the aim of recovering a stack, whose cohomology is exactly
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}
; that is, the dual of the even filtration on
\mathbb{S}[S^{1}]
.
We propose this object to be a spectral lift of the filtered circle in
[
22
]
.
This section is organized as follows.
In §
8.1
, we introduce the synthetic circle as the classifying stack of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
over
\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
.
In §
8.2
, we study the generic fiber of the synthetic circle, recovering the cochain algebra
\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}
for its cohomology.
In §
8.3
, we compute the cohomology of its special fiber.
Finally, in §
8.4
, we describe further deloopings of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
and make some speculative remarks about affine stacks in spectral algebraic geometry.

8.1.
The synthetic circle

Recall,from Construction
3.5
, that given a general grouplike
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid
G\in\operatorname{sStk}_{S}
, we can form its classifying stack over
S
.
We will now specialize to
S=\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
and take
G
to be the synthetic Hilbert additive group.

Definition 8.1
.

The
synthetic circle
is the stack over
\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
given by the classifying stack over
\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
of the synthetic additive group scheme:

S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}=B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}\to\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\,.

Remark 8.2
.

By the compatibility of pullbacks with taking classifying stacks, the synthetic circle fits into the following diagram of pullback squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{grSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}\,.}

8.2.
The generic fiber of the synthetic circle

In
[
22
,
30
]
, it was shown that the stack
K(\mathbb{H},n)
is the
affinization
of the stack
K(\mathbb{Z},n)
for every
n
.
In particular, the natural map
K(\mathbb{Z},n)\to K(\mathbb{H},n)
induces an equivalence on cohomology

R\Gamma(K(\mathbb{H},n),\mathcal{O})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}R\Gamma(K(\mathbb{Z},n),\mathcal{O})\,.

While there is currently no notion of an affine stack in spectral algebraic geometry, we would like to show that working with the spectral Hilbert additive group scheme
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
gives rise to a similar phenomenon in this context.

Theorem 8.3
.

The map
B\mathbb{Z}\to B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
of spectral stacks induces an equivalence

R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}

of bicommutative bialgebras between the cohomology of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
and the spherical cochain algebra
\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}
.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
X
be a spectral geometric stack, with truncation
X_{0}
. Then
f:X\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
is of finite cohomological dimension if and only
f^{\operatorname{cl}}:X_{0}\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
is.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\QCoh(X_{0})^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\leq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\QCoh(X)^{\heartsuit}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Mod_{\mathbb{S}})_{\leq 0}\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\operatorname{cl}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{X}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\iota_{\mathbb{S}}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}}

Both vertical arrows here are
t
-exact since they are actually affine morphisms as they arise as truncation maps of structure sheaves.
Moreover, the left arrow is an equivalence, by Proposition
3.8
.
Thus, we see that for any
\mathcal{F}\in\QCoh(X)^{\heartsuit}\simeq\QCoh(X_{0})^{\heartsuit}
,

f_{*}(\mathcal{F})\in(\Mod_{\mathbb{S}})_{\geq-n}\quad\text{if and only if}\quad f^{\operatorname{cl}}_{*}(\mathcal{F})\in(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})_{\geq-n}\,,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.5
.

The spectral stack
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
is of finite cohomological dimension over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
.

Proof.

By the above lemma,
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
is of finite cohomological dimension over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
if and only if its truncation is.
Its truncation will be a stack over
\mathbb{Z}
which is of finite cohomological dimension by
[
22
, Proposition 3.3.7]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.3
.

Recall that we have a naturally defined map

R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\to\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}

induced by the morphism of spectral abelian group stacks
B\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\to B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
.
Note that the target is bounded below, since it can be written as a finite limit (ranging over the finite CW complex
S^{1}
) of connective spectra.
We will show that the source is itself bounded below as a spectrum. For this, recall that by construction,
R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})
admits an exhaustive filtration with associated graded given by

\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\oplus\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})[-1](-1)

as a module over
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
.
Using this description, we see that
R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})
is itself bounded below.
Indeed, this can be seen inductively on the (finite) layers of the filtration.

Now, given that this is a map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings which are bounded below, we may tensor with
\mathbb{Z}
; this functor is conservative on bounded below spectra and thus may be used to detect equivalences.
We thus obtain a map

(3)

R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H},\mathcal{O})\simeq R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}\,,

where we remind the reader that
\mathbb{H}=\mathrm{Spec}(\Int(\mathbb{Z}))
is the Hilbert additive group over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.
The first equivalence is not immediate and follows from the fact that
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
is of finite cohomological dimension.
Thus, the tensor product of the global sections with
\mathbb{Z}
recovers the global sections of the pullback of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
to
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
which is by construction
B\mathbb{H}
.
Finally, the last equivalence holds since
\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}
is a finite limit which hence commutes with tensoring with
\mathbb{Z}
.
The map given by (
3
) is an equivalence by
[
30
, Corollaire 3.4]
, since
B\mathbb{H}
is the affinization of
B\mathbb{Z}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
and this is just the affinization morphism.
Hence, by conservativity of
\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}
, we conclude that the map in the statement is an equivalence.
∎

8.3.
The special fiber of the synthetic circle

In this section, we study the pullback of the spectral stack
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}=S^{1}_{\operatorname{Syn}}
over the special fiber
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
of
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.

Definition 8.6
.

Let

S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}=S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}B\mathbb{G}_{m}

denote the pullback to
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
of
S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.

Remark 8.7
.

By construction, this pulls back to the
graded circle
of
[
22
]
, or rather its integral version. Namely, the map
\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}\to\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{syn}}
induces a map
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\to\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}
on the associated graded which in turn induces a map

B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{syn}})|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}=:\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))

of spectral stacks over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Now, since pullback commutes with colimits, we may identify

B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times_{\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))}B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq B\mathbb{H}^{\gr}=S^{1}_{\gr}\,.

Being that
S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
gives a lift of
S^{1}_{\gr}
, we would like to study its cohomology, as an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in graded spectra.

Remark 8.8
.

Recall from Section
1.2
that
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-linear
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring.
Thus there is a map

\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}|{Z}

such that the composition

B\mathbb{G}_{m}|{Z}\to\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}|{Z}

is the identity map on
B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Notation 8.9
.

For a stack
f:\mathcal{X}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the pushforward to its structure sheaf
f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in
\QCoh(B\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Via the symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(B\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{S}}^{\gr},

this corresponds to an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in graded spectra.
We use the notation

R\Gamma(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})

to denote this object.

Theorem 8.10
.

There is an equivalence

R\Gamma(S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})\simeq\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\oplus\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})[-1](-1)

of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras in
\Mod_{\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}(\Filt(\Sp))
.
That is, we recover,
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})
as the cohomology of the stack
S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
.

Proof.

It was shown in Proposition
7.9
that the underlying
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-group scheme
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}
is none other than the pullback
\mathsf{Ker}|_{\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}
viewed naturally as a scheme over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Since taking classifying stacks commutes with taking pullbacks, it follows that there is an equivalence

B\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}}\simeq(B\mathsf{Ker})|_{\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})},

where it is understood that the classifying stack on the left is taken relative to the base
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
. Following
[
22
,
27
]
, the cohomology of
B\mathsf{Ker}
over
\mathbb{Z}
is given by

R\Gamma(B\mathsf{Ker},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)

as a graded
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra, i.e. the associated graded of the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
.
Now, it was shown in
[
22
, Lemma 3.4.9]
that
B\mathsf{Ker}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
is of finite cohomological dimension.
We remark that in loc. cit, this was shown over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
for any prime
p
but this is enough to conclude the answer integrally.
Hence, we have an equivalence

R\Gamma(\mathbb{H}^{\gr}_{\mathrm{syn}},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})\simeq R\Gamma(B\mathsf{Ker}|_{\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})\simeq R\Gamma(B\mathsf{Ker},\mathcal{O}^{\gr})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\simeq\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\oplus\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})[-1](-1).

This gives the proof of the desired equivalence.
∎

8.4.
Further deloopings of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
and affine stacks

Since
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-group over
\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
, it can be arbitrarily delooped.
We show in this section that the
n
-fold of its generic fiber
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
recovers the singular cohomology of
B^{n}\mathbb{Z}
, for every
\mathbb{Z}
.

Theorem 8.11
.

The Cartier duality map
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
induces an equivalence on global sections

R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\to R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O}),

for each
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

We show this by induction on
n
.
The
n=1
case is the content of Theorem
8.3
.
Now we assume this is true for
n
, and show it to be true for
n+1
.
By construction there will be a map
B^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}\to B^{n+1}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
of geometric stacks.
In particular there will be an induced morphism of simplicial diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{n}\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{n}\mathbb{Z}\times B^{n}\mathbb{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\times B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

Taking cohomology, we get a morphism of cosimplicial diagrams in
\CAlg
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\times B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z}\times B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

Note that, from topology, we know that

R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z}\times\cdots B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O})\simeq R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O})\otimes\cdots\otimes R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O}).

Moreover, by the induction hypothesis, there is an equivalence

R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\simeq R\Gamma(B^{n}\mathbb{Z},\mathcal{O})

Thus, we just need to show that such a Künneth formula holds for the cohomology of
B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
. We show this for the two fold delooping, the general case can be handled similarly.

It remains therefore to show that there is an equivalence

(4)

R\Gamma(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\times B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\simeq R\Gamma(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\otimes R\Gamma(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})

Note that the left hand side, as a two-fold delooping, may be computed as the totalization of a bi-cosimplicial object, where each
k
th- column computes the cohomology

R\Gamma(\underbrace{B(\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\times\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})\times\cdots\times B(\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\times\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})}_{k\,\operatorname{times}},\mathcal{O}),

that is the global sections of the
k
-fold product of
B(\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\times\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
. Recall that the totalization may be computed by restricting to and totalizing along the diagonal; each term of the diagonal will just be of the form

R\Gamma(\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})^{\times 2m})^{\otimes k}\simeq(\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})})^{\otimes 2mk}

The other side of (
4
) may also be computed by totalizing along the diagonal of a bi-cosimplicial object, there the terms will be of the form

R\Gamma(\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})^{\times m})^{\otimes 2k}\simeq(\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})})^{\otimes 2mk}

Thus, essentially we have reduced both sides to totalizations involving tensor products of cohomologies of affine schemes, which agree because
\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
, being affine, satisfies the Künneth formula on global sections. Thus both cosimplicial objects are equivalent, and hence so will be their totalizations.
∎

We now speculate a bit about the notion of affine stack in spectral algebraic geometry.
This is of course a bit of a naïve, yet to be defined, notion.
However, whatever class of stacks this eventually is, we expect that it contains the collection of deloopings
\{B^{n}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\}
for all
n>0
.
We give some evidence for these expectations, thus elucidating a key feature of affine stacks that we expect will generalize to spectral algebraic geometry.

Let
A=R\Gamma(BG,\mathcal{O})
be an augmented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra, which we would like to think of as the global sections of some classifying spectral stack
BG
. Recall from
[
17
, Section 5.2]
, the bar–cobar adjunction

\operatorname{Bar}:\operatorname{CAlg}_{/1}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{coAlg}_{1/}:\operatorname{Cobar}

between augmented algebras and (co)augmented coalgebras.
One might ask when this is an equivalence.
For example, when working over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})
and
A
is the cohomology of an affine stack, then the natural map

A\to\operatorname{Cobar}(\operatorname{Bar}(A))

will be an equivalence by definition.
In particular, suppose we are working in the filtered setting, and
A=\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
, that is, the Postnikov filtration on
\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}
.
Then we recover the description

\operatorname{Cobar}(\operatorname{Bar}(\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}))\simeq R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}^{\fil},\mathcal{O}^{\fil})\simeq\tau_{\geq\bullet}\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}

of cosimplicial commutative algebras in filtered
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

The
\mathbb{S}
-linear version of the above equivalence for
A=\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}
, as in Section
2.1
, will also hold.
This follows from our definition of the synthetic Hilbert additive group as a bar construction on
\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}
and by our computations of the cohomology of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.
Furthermore, this can be iterated, by Theorem
8.11
, so that the iterated bar–cobar adjunction of
[
17
]
is an equivalence as well.
After all, this is how we were led to the eventual definition of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.

Thus, we can at least say that a necessary condition for a class of algebras
\mathcal{C}
to correspond to the correct notion of affine stacks is to be such that the unit of the bar–cobar adjunction is an equivalence for every
A\in\mathcal{C}
.
One can also demand that, when applicable, the unit of the iterated bar–cobar adjunction
A\to\operatorname{Cobar}^{(k)}\operatorname{Bar}^{(k)}(A)
is an equivalence as well.

9.
Representations of the Synthetic Circle

In this section, we study the
\infty
-category of representations of the synthetic circle.
In §
9.1
, we systematically study the
\infty
-categories of quasicoherent sheaves on the deloopings
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
and
B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.
In the former case, we obtain a quasi-coherent sheaf model for filtered unipotent
\mathbb{Z}
-representations.
In the latter case, we obtain lifts of the
\infty
-category of
S^{1}_{\fil}
-representations studied in
[
22
]
to the synthetic world.
In particular, we obtain a filtration on the
\infty
-category of
S^{1}
-equivariant spectra.
Finally, in §
9.2
, we describe a filtration on topological Hochschild homology obtained via the synthetic circle, and informally discuss its relationship with the motivic filtration on THH.

9.1.
Representations of the synthetic circle

In
[
22
]
, a systematic investigation was undertaken of the
\infty
-category of representations of the filtered circle. The structure of
S^{1}_{\fil}
as an abelian group scheme over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
was shown to give rise to a degeneration, at the level of symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-categories

\Fun(BS^{1},\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}})\leadsto\operatorname{coMod}_{\mathbb{Z}[d]}

from
S^{1}
-equivariant
\mathbb{Z}
-modules to the stable
\infty
-category of graded mixed complexes
7
7

7

Homotopy coherent chain complexes in the language of
[
27
]
.
In this section, we initiate a study of the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves on
B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
, and show that it gives a similar degeneration between
S^{1}
-equivariant spectra and graded mixed complexes with coefficients in
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
.
First, we would like to show that
\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
gives a model for unipotent local systems on the topological space
B\mathbb{Z}\simeq S^{1}
.

Proposition 9.1
.

There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\,.

Proof.

First, we note that, by finite cohomological dimension, the unit is a compact object, and that, moreover, global sections commutes with filtered colimits by
[
19
, Proposition 9.1.5.3]
.
Recall that, by construction, there is an equivalence

R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},\mathcal{O})\simeq\hom_{\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})}(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{1})\simeq\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}.

Thus, if we can show that every object
E\in\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
is generated by colimits of the unit object, then we are done.

We now show that if
E\in\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
is bounded below, then
E\simeq 0
if the push-forward
\pi_{*}(E)\simeq 0
, for
\pi:B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\to\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
.
This tells us that global sections is conservative on bounded below objects.
We will prove this claim by reducing it to the well-known fact that the functor
(-)\otimes{S}\mathbb{Z}:\Sp\to\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
is conservative when restricted to bounded below spectra.
For this, first note that, by finite cohomological dimension of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
, the diagram of pullbacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sp}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Mod{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\QCoh(B\mathbb{H})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\pi}^{*}}

is right-adjointable, so that there is an equivalence

\iota^{*}\circ\pi_{*}\simeq\tilde{\pi}_{*}\circ\tilde{\iota}^{*}\,.

Hence, since the global sections functor
\tilde{\pi}_{*}:\QCoh(B\mathbb{H})\to\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}
is already known to be conservative, as
\QCoh(B\mathbb{H})\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}^{S^{1}}}
, this implies that conservativity of
\pi_{*}
is equivalent to the conservativity of the functor
\tilde{i}^{*}:\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})\to\QCoh(B\mathbb{H})
.
To see conservativity of the functor
\tilde{i}^{*}
, note from the following Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}}

that conservativity of
\tilde{\iota}^{*}
(on bounded below objects) follows from conservativity of the other three induced pullback functors.
Indeed,
e^{*}
and
\tilde{e}^{*}
are conservative since they are flat covers, and
\iota^{*}\simeq(-)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z}
which again, is conservative on bounded below spectra.

We have shown that if
E
is bounded below that
\hom(\mathbf{1},E)\simeq\pi_{*}(E)\simeq 0
, implies
E\simeq 0
where
\mathbf{1}
denotes the unit in
\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
.
Thus every bounded below object is generated via shifts and colimits of
\mathbf{1}
. Now finally let
E
be arbitrary.
By right-completeness of the t-structure on
\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
we may write
E\simeq\colim_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\tau_{\geq n}(E)
.
But each
\tau_{\geq n}(E)
is itself a colimit of
\mathbf{1}
, allowing us to conclude that
\mathbf{1}
itself sits in the subcategory generated by
\mathbf{1}
.
∎

Remark 9.2
.

If we are to think of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
as an affinization of
B\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
over
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{S})
, then Toën’s theory of affine stacks predicts that
\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
should only keep track of
unipotent
representations of
\mathbb{Z}
(see e.g.
[
21
, Definition 7.7]
).
The above proposition confirms this expectation.
Indeed, by
[
21
