, Proposition 7.8]
, the localizing subcategory

\Mod_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}\subset\Fun(S^{1},\Sp)

generated by the unit
\mathbf{1}
in
S^{1}
-parametrized spectra is identified with the category of parametrized spectra
M
for which for all
x\in S^{1}
the action of
\pi_{1}(S^{1},x)\cong\mathbb{Z}
on the fiber
M_{x}
is ind-unipotent.

We now state one of the main theorems of the section which recovers
S^{1}
-equivariant spectra as quasi-coherent sheaves on
B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
.

Theorem 9.3
.

There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\QCoh(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})\simeq\Sp^{BS^{1}}

induced by applying
\QCoh(-)
to the map
B^{2}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\to B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
of stacks.

Proof.

The argument is essentially verbatim the same as in the proof of the
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-analogue in
[
22
, Proposition 4.2.3]
.
One begins with the fact that both
B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
and
B^{2}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
are presented as geometric realizations of simplicial diagrams

[n]\mapsto B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\times n}\,\,\,\,\,\,\mathrm{and}\,\,\,\,\,\,[n]\mapsto B\underline{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times n}

with a map
u_{\bullet}:B\underline{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times\bullet}\to B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\times\bullet}
.
Applying
\QCoh(-)
to these simplicial diagrams gives a map of cosimplicial objects in
\Pr^{L}_{\mathrm{St}}
, which in degree
n
is given by

u_{n}^{*}:\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}^{\times n})\simeq\Mod_{(\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}})^{\otimes n}}\to\Fun((S^{1})^{\times n},\Sp)\simeq\QCoh((S^{1})^{\times n}\,.

We would like to show that each
u_{n}^{*}
is fully faithful.
This will follow from the fact that the constant diagram
\underline{\mathbb{S}}\in\Fun((S^{1})^{\times n},\Sp)
is a compact object, which is true since each
(S^{1})^{n}
is a compact space.
One uses this to check that the unit map of the adjunction

M\mapsto(u_{n})_{*}u_{n}^{*}(M)

is an equivalence, where the right adjoint is given by taking the limit over
(S^{1})^{\times n}
of a parametrized spectrum over
(S^{1})^{\times n}
.
By compactness of the constant diagram, the right adjoint itself commutes with colimits, so it amounts to verifying the above equivalence
for
N\simeq(\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}})^{\otimes n}
from which it follows from the fact that
u_{n}^{*}
is symmetric monoidal and so sends
N
to the constant diagram
\underline{\mathbb{S}}
.

We now check essential surjectivity.
We recall that since
u^{*}
is the limit of fully faithful functors
u_{n}^{*}
’s, on each degree, an object
(E_{n})_{n\in\Delta}
on the right hand side is in the essential image if and only if each
E_{n}
is in the image of
u^{*}_{n}
.
However, each
E_{n}
is obtained as a pullback (along the simplicial structure maps) of
E_{0}\in\Sp
where
u_{0}
is obviously an equivalence.
By the compatibility of the pullbacks
u_{n}^{*}
with the cosimplicial structure maps, we conclude that
E_{n}
is in the essential image
u_{n}^{*}
giving us essential surjectivity.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

The above theorem says that the
\infty
-category
\QCoh(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})
captures all
\mathbb{S}
-linear
representations of the
\infty
-group
S^{1}\simeq B\mathbb{Z}
.
Contrast this to the
n=1
case, where
\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}})\simeq\Mod_{\mathbb{S}^{S^{1}}}
, thus capturing only locally unipotent representations of
\mathbb{Z}
.
This aligns with the paradigm from the theory of affine stacks in the
\mathbb{Z}
-linear case, where the affinization of a space keeps track of the unipotent part of the fundamental group, i.e for
B\mathbb{Z}\to B\mathbb{H}
, we only recover
\mathbb{H}
-representations.
The space
B^{2}\mathbb{Z}\simeq\mathbb{C}P^{\infty}
is of course simply connected, so we recover all
B\mathbb{Z}
-equivariant spectra.

Now we analyze quasi-coherent sheaves on deloopings of
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}
.

Construction 9.5
.

\mathcal{C}
be a stable, presentably symmetric monoidal category and let
A\in\operatorname{bAlg}(\mathcal{C})
denote a bicommutative bialgebra in
\mathcal{C}
. By

[
17
, Corollary 4.7.5.3]
(see also
[
23
, Proposition 4.6]
for the relevant comonadic version) the

\infty
-category of

From the above, given the fact that each term is symmetric monoidal and that this limit can be taken in
\CAlg(\Pr^{L}_{\mathrm{St}})
, we deduce a symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{coMod}_{A}(\mathcal{C})
.

We summarize with the following theorem.

Theorem 9.6
.

There are symmetric monoidal equivalences

(5)

\QCoh(S^{1}_{\mathrm{syn}}|_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}})=\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})\simeq\Mod_{R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})}\simeq\Mod_{\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})}

(6)

\QCoh(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})\simeq\operatorname{coMod}_{\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})}(\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})})

Here, the symmetric monoidal structure on the right hand side of (
5
) is just the relative tensor product over
R\Gamma(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})
.
The symmetric monoidal structure on the right hand side of (
6
) is the one described in Construction
9.5
.

Proof.

For the first equivalence, the key fact we use is the consequence of Proposition
7.9
, stating that

\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}\simeq\mathsf{Ker}\times_{\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{Z})}\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))

so that
\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}
is just the base-change of

\mathsf{Ker}\simeq\mathbb{H}^{t=0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}

to
\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))
.
Equivalently, since everything here is relatively affine, this can be written as an equivalence of

(\mathbb{S}_{\Int(\mathbb{Z})}^{\mathrm{syn}})^{\gr}\simeq\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})

in
\CAlg(\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})})
, i.e. in
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
-algebras in graded
\mathbb{Z}
-modules.

Now, let
X=B\mathsf{Ker}
as a stack over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}=B\mathbb{G}_{m}|_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
We remark that the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
arises as a totalization

\QCoh(X)\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\hbox to206.76pt{\vbox to35.32pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 103.37675pt\lower-17.65974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-103.37675pt}{-17.65974pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -143.04 -24.44)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}
{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{{}}}{{{}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}

arising from an fpqc cover
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to X
.
The desired equivalence will follow if we can demonstrate an equivalence

(7)

\QCoh\left(X\times_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr_{\mathrm{ev}}^{*}(\mathbb{S}))\right)\simeq\QCoh(X)\otimes^{L}_{\Mod^{\gr}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}\,,

where the right hand side is the Lurie tensor product in
\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr}
-linear presentable stable
\infty
-categories, which we denote by
\Pr^{L}_{\mathrm{St}}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr})
.
Indeed, for
X=B\mathsf{Ker}
, this will mean that

\QCoh(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})\simeq\QCoh\left(B\mathsf{Ker}\times_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}\gr\mathrm{Spec}(\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S}))\right)\simeq\Mod_{R\Gamma(B\mathsf{Ker},\mathcal{O})}\otimes_{\Mod^{\gr}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}\,,

and for
X=B^{2}\mathsf{Ker}
this will mean that

\QCoh(B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr})\simeq\operatorname{coMod}_{\mathbb{Z}[d]}(\Mod^{\gr}{Z})\otimes^{L}\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}\simeq\operatorname{coMod}_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})[d]}(\Gr(\Mod{Z}))\,.

Now let us demonstrate the equivalence (
7
).
We remark that since
\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})
is a
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in graded
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, the
\infty
-category
\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}
is a dualizable object, so tensoring with respect to it will commute with small limits in
\Pr^{L}_{\mathrm{St}}(\Mod_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\gr})
.
Hence, we have an equivalence

\displaystyle\QCoh(X)\otimes^{L}_{\Mod^{\gr}_{\mathbb{Z}}}\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}

\displaystyle\simeq\lim_{\Delta}\left(\begin{aligned} \hbox to161.09pt{\vbox to35.32pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 80.54352pt\lower-17.65974pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Here, the second equivalence is the aforementioned dualizability of
\Mod_{\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})}
and the second equivalence just follows from the relevant Künneth formulas which are valid in this context (as in the proof of Theorem
8.11
).
Hence, the equivalence (
7
) holds and we conclude the first equivalence in the statement.

Now we prove the second equivalence using the first one.

arising from the fpqc cover
\gr\mathrm{Spec}\gr^{*}_{\mathrm{ev}}(\mathbb{S})\to B^{2}\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}
.
We just need equivalences

\Mod_{(\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee}))^{\otimes n}}\to\QCoh(({B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\gr}})^{\times n})

in each cosimplicial degree compatible with the cosimplicial structure maps.
But there always exist natural transformations from modules over the global sections to quasi-coherent sheaves, which by the first part of this proof, will be equivalences in this case.
∎

Remark 9.7
.

Recall from
[
22
]
that the
\infty
-category
\QCoh(B^{2}\mathsf{Ker})
is equivalent to
graded mixed complexes
, that is graded complexes
M^{n}
equipped with a “differential”

\delta:M^{n}[1]\to M^{n+1}

which squares to zero.
This differential corresponds to a strict coaction by the graded Hopf algebra
\mathbb{Z}\oplus\mathbb{Z}[-1](-1)
.
The above theorem displays a lift of graded mixed complexes to the special fiber of even synthetic spectra, as comodules over
\gr^{*}(\mathbb{T}_{\mathrm{syn}}^{\vee})
. However, the relation
\eta d=d^{2}
in
\pi_{*}(\mathbb{S}[S^{1}])
implies that
\delta
does not square to zero. We expect that, in spite of the fact that we are now working in
\mathbb{Z}
-modules, these “
\eta
-deformed mixed complexes” retain interesting homotopical information.

9.2.
Filtration on Topological Hochschild Homology

We conclude with a few speculative remarks about filtrations on topological Hochschild homology.
Recall that in
[
22
]
, the HKR filtration on Hochschild homology was geometrized in terms of derived mapping stacks out of the filtered circle; as such, it acquired a universal property as an derived commutative algebra object in
S^{1}_{\fil}
-representations.
We will see that the constructions of this paper give rise to a filtration on topological Hochschild homology for connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in a similar manner.

Construction 9.8
.

Let
X=\mathrm{Spec}(A)
, for
A
as above.
Then we may form the mapping stack

\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{syn}}(X)=\Map_{\operatorname{sStk}_{/\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})}}(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}},X\times\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}}))\to\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\,.

As this is a relative construction over
\operatorname{fSpec}(\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{syn}})
, it admits a map to
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Hence by the yoga of filtrations and quasi-coherent sheaves over
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, one obtains a filtration on the filtration of the generic fiber of
\mathcal{L}^{\mathrm{syn}}(X)
.

Arguing exactly as in
[
25
, Theorem 9.9]
, we obtain the following result:

Proposition 9.9
.

Let
X=\mathrm{Spec}(A)
be an affine spectral scheme.
Then the map

B\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\underline{S^{1}}\to B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}

induces an equivalence

\mathrm{THH}(A)\simeq R\Gamma(\underline{\Map}(S^{1},X))\simeq R\Gamma(\underline{\Map}(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},X))

Moreover, the
S^{1}
-action on
\mathrm{THH}(A)
corresponds to the
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}}
action on
R\Gamma(\underline{\Map}(B\mathbb{H}_{\mathbb{S}},X))
via the equivalence of categories of Theorem
9.3
.

By putting Construction
9.8
and Proposition
9.9
together, one obtains the desired filtration on
\mathrm{THH}(A)
.

Remark 9.10
.

We do not expect this filtration to recover the motivic filtration on topological Hochschild cohomology, when defined.
Indeed, this can already be seen at the level of Hochschild homology, since motivic filtrations are compatible with the HKR filtration on Hochschild homology when forming the tensor product
-\otimes_{\mathrm{THH}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{Z}
.
Let
A\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{cn}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and
\mathrm{THH}^{\fil}(A)
be the resulting
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in filtered spectra.
By extension of scalars to
\mathbb{Z}
, one obtains a filtration of
\HH(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}\mathbb{Z})
.
This filtration also arises, via the Rees equivalence, as the pushforward to
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
of the structure sheaf of

\Map_{\mathbb{Z}}(S^{1}_{\fil},X|_{\mathbb{Z}}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\,.

This mapping stack is taken inside derived stacks based on
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras.
In
[
24
, Conjecture 9.2]
, it was conjectured that one obtains the same
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra of functions, regardless if one takes the mapping stack internally to the animated ring setting, or the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra setting.
However, this is false; one can see this on the associated graded.
On the simplicial commutative ring side one obtains as global sections
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{*}(L_{A|\mathbb{Z}}[1](1))
; on
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-derived geometry one obtains
\operatorname{Sym}^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}(L^{\operatorname{top}}_{A|\mathbb{Z}}[1])
.
Here,
\mathrm{Sym}^{*}
and
\operatorname{Sym}^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}
denote the derived symmetric algebra monad and
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra monad respectively, and the
L_{A|\mathbb{Z}}[1]
and
L^{\operatorname{top}}_{A|\mathbb{Z}}[1]
denote the algebraic and topological cotangent complex respectively.
These are different hence it is typically the case that

\operatorname{Sym}^{*}_{\mathbb{E}_{\infty}}(L^{\operatorname{top}}_{A|\mathbb{Z}}[1])\nsimeq\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Sym}^{*}(L_{A|\mathbb{Z}}[1](1))\,.

Putting all this together, we see that, when
A
is an animated commutative ring, the filtration on
\mathrm{THH}(A)
obtained here geometrically will not recover the motivic filtrations of
[
4
]
and
[
3
]
.

Remark 9.11
.

In light of the above remark, we expect the synthetic circle
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
constructed here to still be useful in defining a universal property for the motivic filtration on
\mathrm{THH}
.
In particular, we expect that, by working in a different variant of algebraic geometry over the sphere spectrum, not based on connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras but instead based on
T
-algebras for some monad
T
on synthetic spectra, that the motivic filtration on THH can be geometrized by way of
B\mathbb{H}_{\mathrm{syn}}
.
